> DEVELOPER

let's investigate at #1 todo, and also keep in mind I'd like to
create trait(s) it reduce the boiler plate to invoke a subcommand
and manage it's help.

On the invocation we have this repeated for every invocation now:
        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => {
            let ctx = match context::Context::load(cli.log) {
                Ok(c) => c,
                Err(e) => {
                    error!("{e}");
                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
                }
            };
            let params = match chid::ChidParams::try_from(&chid_args) {
                Ok(p) => p,
                Err(e) => {
                    error!("{e}");
                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
                }
            };
            run_command(chid::chid(&ctx, &params))
        }

35	/// Banner string emitted as the first line of normal command runs
36	/// and shown at the top of subcommand `--help` output. Built from
37	/// `Cargo.toml`'s name + version at compile time so it stays in
38	/// sync with the bumped version.
39	const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
40	
41	/// Top-level about line — name, version, and the project tagline
42	/// on a single line. Used as the top-level `about` so `vc-x1 -h`
43	/// reads as one banner-plus-tagline header instead of two stacked
44	/// lines.
45	const TOP_ABOUT: &str = concat!(
46	    env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"),
47	    " ",
48	    env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
49	    " - jj workspace tooling"
50	);
51	
52	/// Build the clap command tree with `BANNER` set as `before_help`
53	/// on every subcommand (transitively). Top-level skips `before_help`
54	/// because its own `about` already carries the name+version+tagline.
55	/// Walks via `mut_subcommand` so individual subcommand
56	/// `#[command(long_about = ...)]` blocks don't have to repeat the
57	/// banner text.
58	fn cli_with_banner() -> clap::Command {
59	    fn add_to_subs(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
60	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
61	            .get_subcommands()
62	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
63	            .collect();
64	        for name in names {
65	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
66	        }
67	        cmd
68	    }
69	    fn add_with_banner(mut cmd: clap::Command) -> clap::Command {
70	        cmd = cmd.before_help(BANNER);
71	        let names: Vec<String> = cmd
72	            .get_subcommands()
73	            .map(|c| c.get_name().to_string())
74	            .collect();
75	        for name in names {
76	            cmd = cmd.mut_subcommand(name, add_with_banner);
77	        }
78	        cmd
79	    }
80	    add_to_subs(Cli::command())
81	}

132	        Status labels:\n  \

134	          fix   — ochid has issues, shows proposed fix (dry-run)\n  \

151	    /// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes
152	    #[command(long_about = "Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n\n\
153	        Repo set is resolved (in order):\n  \
154	          - `-R` / `--repo`     exact list (back-compat / arbitrary multi-repo)\n  \
155	          - `--scope=code|bot|code,bot` dual-repo roles via `.vc-config.toml`\n  \
156	          - neither             default: `code,bot` when dual, else `code`\n\n\
157	        Default is --check (verify only) — fatal if any repo needs\n\
158	        action. Re-run with --no-check to apply (rebase / fast-forward).\n\
159	        Per repo:\n  \
160	          - up-to-date        nothing to do\n  \
161	          - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote\n  \
162	          - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)\n  \
163	          - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts\n  \
164	          - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip\n\n\
165	        Scripts and automation should pass --check or --no-check\n\
166	        explicitly — defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the\n\
167	        contract. Interactive use can rely on the default.\n\n\
168	        After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly\n\
169	        advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing\n\
170	        working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up\n\
171	        orphaned on a stale branch.\n\n\
172	        On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via\n\
173	        `jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the\n\
174	        revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.\n\n\
175	        Output shape:\n  \
176	          - all-up-to-date: one-line summary (`sync: N repos, all bookmarks up-to-date`)\n  \
177	          - action needed (--check):  per-repo fetch + state + fatal error\n  \
178	          - action needed (--no-check): per-repo fetch + state + actions\n  \
179	          - --quiet:        no output; exit code signals success")]
180	    Sync(sync::SyncArgs),
181

184	        Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:\n\
185	        --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,\n\
186	        --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.\n\
187	        Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.")]
188	    Finalize(finalize::FinalizeArgs),

190	    /// Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command
191	    #[command(
192	        long_about = "Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.\n\n\
193	        Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a\n\
194	        single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a\n\
195	        state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can\n\
196	        resume without re-doing completed stages.\n\n\
197	        Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)\n\
198	        → message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →\n\
199	        commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both\n\
200	        → push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /\n\
201	        commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via\n\
202	        `jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.\n\
203	        After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and\n\
204	        recovery is forward-only.\n\n\
205	        Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes\n\
206	        to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body\n\
207	        across resumes so only the first invocation needs them."
208	    )]
209	    Push(push::PushArgs),
210	}

212	fn run_command(result: Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>) -> ExitCode {
213	    match result {
214	        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
215	        Err(e) => {
216	            error!("{e}");
217	            ExitCode::FAILURE
218	        }
219	    }
220	}
221	
222	/// Permanent sanity check for the `main`-bookmark tracking state
223	/// in both repos of the dual-repo workspace. Prints one line on
224	/// entry and one on exit of every command. If entry and exit
225	/// differ, the executing command is the culprit; if entry differs
226	/// from the previous command's exit, something *between*
227	/// invocations broke it. Originally added in 0.37.2 as a temporary
228	/// diagnostic; promoted to permanent in 0.37.4 after the user
229	/// reported "happens more than once" — the signal is worth keeping
230	/// in place until confidence in its correctness is well-established
231	/// across varied use, at which point a "silent when clean"
232	/// refinement would remove the steady-state noise without losing
233	/// detection value (see `notes/todo.md`).
234	///
235	/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory
236	/// whose `.vc-config.toml` has `path = "/"`), then probes `<root>`
237	/// and `<root>/.claude`. Same labeling whether the user runs from
238	/// the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.
239	fn bm_track(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
240	    let header = format!("bm-track {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}");
241	    let root = match common::find_workspace_root() {
242	        Some(r) => r,
243	        None => {
244	            log::info!("{header}: no-workspace");
245	            return;
246	        }
247	    };
248	    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] =
249	        [(root.clone(), "app"), (root.join(".claude"), ".claude")];
250	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
251	    for (repo, label) in repos {
252	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
253	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
254	            continue;
255	        }
256	        match bm_track_one(&repo, "main", "origin") {
257	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
258	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
259	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
260	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
261	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
262	            )),
263	        }
264	    }
265	    log::info!("{header}: {}", parts.join(", "));
266	}
267	
268	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
269	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows
270	/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or
271	/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both
272	/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists
273	/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on
274	/// subprocess failure.
275	fn bm_track_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
276	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
277	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
278	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
279	        .output()
280	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
281	    if !output.status.success() {
282	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
283	    }
284	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
285	    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated
286	    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still
287	    // represents a tracking relationship.
288	    let colon = format!("@{remote}:");
289	    let paren = format!("@{remote} ");
290	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {
291	        let t = l.trim_start();
292	        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)
293	    }))
294	}
295	
296	fn main() -> ExitCode {
297	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
298	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
299	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
300	        Ok(cli) => cli,
301	        Err(e) => {
302	            e.exit();
303	        }
304	    };
305	
306	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
307	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
308	
309	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
310	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
311	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
312	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
313	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
314	        cli.command,
315	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
316	    );
317	
318	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
319	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
320	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
321	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
322	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
323	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
324	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
325	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
326	        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
327	        _ => false,
328	    };
329	
330	    if !is_detached_exec {
331	        if !suppress_banner {
332	            // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what `--help`
333	            // shows at the top.
334	            log::info!("{BANNER}");
335	        }
336	        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
337	    }
338	
339	    // Command name for bm-track output (first positional arg after
340	    // the binary; clap has already validated it by the time we get here).
341	    let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
342	
343	    if !is_detached_exec {
344	        bm_track("enter", &command_name);
345	    }
346	
347	    let exit_code = match cli.command {
348	        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => {
349	            let ctx = match context::Context::load(cli.log) {
350	                Ok(c) => c,
351	                Err(e) => {
352	                    error!("{e}");

354	                }

362	            };

371	                }

--- chid.rs head ---
//! `chid` subcommand — print the short change ID of each commit in a
//! revision range, one per line (script-friendly output).
//!
//! - `ChidArgs`: clap surface; flattens
//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.
//! - `ChidParams`: clap-free; embeds `common::CommonParams`. Built
//!   via `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` at the binary edge.
//! - `chid(&Context, &ChidParams)`: the op — `for_each_repo` + print
//!   `format_chid`.

use clap::Args;
use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
use log::{debug, info};

use crate::common::{self, CommonParams};
use crate::context::Context;
use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;

/// CLI args for `chid` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct ChidArgs {
    #[command(flatten)]
    pub common: CommonArgs,
}

/// Clap-free params for `chid`; embeds resolved `CommonParams`.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ChidParams {
    pub common: CommonParams,
}

impl TryFrom<&ChidArgs> for ChidParams {
    type Error = String;

    /// Resolve clap `ChidArgs` into `ChidParams` by delegating to
    /// `CommonParams::try_from`; `chid` has no fields beyond
    /// `CommonArgs`.
    fn try_from(a: &ChidArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {
        Ok(ChidParams {
            common: CommonParams::try_from(&a.common)?,
        })
    }
}

/// Print the short change ID of each commit in the resolved range.
///
/// `_ctx` is unused (chid has no user-config or `--log` consumer);
/// it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
pub fn chid(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    debug!("chid: enter");
    let c = &params.common;
    common::for_each_repo(&c.repos, &c.header, |workspace, repo| {
        let (ids, _) = common::collect_ids(
            workspace,
            repo,
            &c.spec.rev,
            c.spec.desc_count,
            c.spec.anc_count,
        )?;
        for commit_id in &ids {
            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
            info!("{}", common::format_chid(&commit));
        }
        Ok(())
    })?;
    debug!("chid: exit");
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use std::path::PathBuf;

    use clap::Parser;

    use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
    use crate::{Cli, Commands};

    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> CommonArgs {
        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
        match cli.command {
            Commands::Chid(a) => a.common,
            _ => panic!("expected Chid"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn defaults() {
        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid"]);
        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@");
        assert_eq!(c.repo, None);
        assert_eq!(c.scope, None);
        assert_eq!(c.limit, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn with_revision() {
        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-r", "@-"]);
        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@-");
    }
--- show.rs head ---
//! `show` subcommand — for each commit in a revision range, print
//! author / committer / ids / parents / children / branches /
//! follows / precedes / description body / a changed-files summary.
//!
//! - `ShowArgs`: clap surface; flattens
//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs` plus a `-f`/`--files`
//!   cap (raw `String`; parsed at the boundary into [`FileLimit`]).
//! - `ShowParams`: clap-free; embeds `common::CommonParams` and the
//!   parsed `FileLimit`. Built via `TryFrom<&ShowArgs>` at the
//!   binary edge.
//! - `show(&Context, &ShowParams)`: the op — `for_each_repo`, one
//!   `show_one_commit` per commit.

use std::sync::Arc;

use chrono::Offset;
use clap::Args;
use jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;
use jj_lib::commit::Commit;
use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
use jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;
use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
use jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;
use pollster::FutureExt;

use log::{debug, info};

use crate::common::{self, CommonParams};
use crate::context::Context;
use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;

/// Parsed file limit: None (suppress), Some(n) (cap at n), or all.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FileLimit {
    /// Don't show changed files section at all.
    None,
    /// Show up to N files (with first/last split if truncated).
    Cap(usize),
    /// Show all files.
    All,
}

impl FileLimit {
    /// Parse the `--files` flag string into a `FileLimit` — `"0"` →
    /// None, `"all"` → All, otherwise a positive integer → Cap(n).
    /// Called at the boundary by `ShowParams::try_from`.
    pub fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
        match s {
            "0" => Ok(FileLimit::None),
            "all" => Ok(FileLimit::All),
            _ => s
                .parse::<usize>()
                .map(FileLimit::Cap)
                .map_err(|_| format!("invalid file limit '{s}': expected number, 0, or 'all'")),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct ShowArgs {
    #[command(flatten)]
    pub common: CommonArgs,

    /// Max changed files: number, 0 (none), or 'all'
    #[arg(short = 'f', long = "files", default_value = "50")]
    pub files: String,
}

/// Clap-free params for `show`; embeds `CommonParams` plus the parsed
/// `FileLimit`.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ShowParams {
    pub common: CommonParams,
    pub files: FileLimit,
}

impl TryFrom<&ShowArgs> for ShowParams {
    type Error = String;

1	# options_flags — Reusable CLI Options and Flags
2	
3	Per-flag CLI surfaces shared across multiple subcommands. Each
4	shared option or flag (OF) lives in its own submodule so its
5	type, parser, and tests stay together.
6	
7	This module is the CLI-layer leaf store for the options_flags
8	extraction in [`../../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../../ARCHITECTURE.md);
9	see there for how the leaves fit the wider clap-args /
10	`Context`+`Params` split.
11	
12	## Architecture
13	
14	Three composition patterns:
15	
16	- **Leaf** — a `#[derive(Args)]` struct holding the flag(s),
17	  `value_parser`, default, and help text (via the field
18	  doc-comment). One `#[derive(Args)]` per logical OF — single
19	  flag or a small fixed pair (e.g. `push_retry`'s two fields).
20	  Help text aims to be generic enough for any reasonable
21	  consumer.
22	- **Bundle** — a `#[derive(Args)]` shared by N subcommands,
23	  picked up with one `#[command(flatten)]` line. Two forms:
24	  - *flatten-of-leaves* — composes existing leaves into a named
25	    role (e.g. `ProvisionOptionFlagBundle` = `dry_run` +
26	    `private` + `push_retry`). Use this when the constituent
27	    flags are themselves reused elsewhere.
28	  - *inline-fields* — holds the `#[arg]` fields directly (e.g.
29	    `common_args::CommonArgs` — the read-only commit-query arg
30	    set). Use this when a whole *set* of args is shared but the
31	    individual flags aren't reused outside the bundle —
32	    extracting per-flag leaves would buy no reuse. If one of the
33	    fields ever *is* reused (a future command wants just that
34	    flag), extract it into a leaf then and
35	    `#[command(flatten)]` it into the bundle.
36	- **Pattern A escape hatch** — when a consumer needs unique help
37	  text (or different defaults), it skips the leaf's flatten and
38	  inlines its own `#[arg(value_parser = …)]` field, reusing the
39	  leaf's typed value and parser.
40	
41	## Flag vs Option — classify by domain, not wire syntax
42	
43	Every leaf falls into one of two categories, picked by the value
44	domain it carries:
45	
46	- **Flag** — boolean domain. Wire form may be presence/absence
47	  (`--dry-run`), explicit negation (`--no-dry-run`), or value
48	  (`--dry-run=true|false`). All three express a `bool`. Type
49	  name suffix: `*Flag`.
50	- **Option** — non-boolean domain (string, integer, enum, path,
51	  …). Wire form is always `--name=<value>` (or `--name <value>`).
52	  Type name suffix: `*Option` (or `*Options` for a multi-field
53	  leaf like `PushRetryOptions`).
54	
55	Wire form is presentation; the category is the underlying domain.
56	
57	## Adding a new leaf
58	
59	1. Create `options_flags/<name>.rs` with a
60	   `#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]` struct.
61	2. Pick the suffix per domain: `*Flag` (boolean) or `*Option(s)`
62	   (non-boolean).
63	3. Name the field(s):
64	   - **Single-field leaf** — call the field `value` (it holds the
65	     parsed value-side of the option) and declare the flag
66	     explicitly with `#[arg(long = "<flag>", …)]`. The consumer
67	     then reads `args.<leaf>.value` rather than doubling the flag
68	     name (`args.squash.value`, not `args.squash.squash`).
69	     Caveat: clap derives an arg's *id* from the field name, so
70	     if two `value`-field leaves can ever be flattened into the
71	     same struct, give each an explicit `#[arg(id = "<unique>")]`
72	     (else `clap_builder` panics — duplicate arg id `value`).
73	   - **Multi-field leaf** — descriptive per-field names
74	     (`push_retries`, `push_retry_delay`); clap derives each
75	     flag from its field name as usual.
76	   - The pre-existing single-field leaves (`scope`, `repo`,
77	     `dry_run`, `private`, `account`, `config`, `use_template`)
78	     predate this and still derive the flag from the field name;
79	     migrating them to `value` is tracked in
80	     [`../../notes/todo.md`](../../notes/todo.md).
81	4. Add `pub mod <name>;` to `mod.rs`.
82	5. If the leaf has explicit parsing logic, declare a unit-struct
83	   implementor of `FlagParser` (boolean domain) or `OptionParser`
84	   (non-boolean domain). Bare `Option<String>` leaves and
85	   presence/absence boolean leaves need no parser impl — clap
86	   handles them.
87	6. Add tests for any non-trivial parser/resolver logic.
88	7. Leaves do **not** implement a Bundle marker — bundles do.
89	
90	## Consuming an OF
91	
92	Default — flatten the leaf into your subcommand's `Args`:
93	
94	```rust
95	#[derive(Args)]
96	pub struct MyArgs {
97	    #[command(flatten)]
98	    pub config: ConfigOption,
99	    // ...
100	}
101	```
102	
103	Pattern A — when generic help doesn't fit:
104	
105	```rust
106	#[derive(Args)]
107	pub struct MyArgs {
108	    /// My subcommand-specific help for --config.
109	    #[arg(long = "config", value_name = "none|PATH",
110	          verbatim_doc_comment)]
111	    pub config: Option<String>,
112	    // ...
113	}
114	```
115	
116	A Pattern A consumer reuses the leaf's types (e.g. `ConfigKind`)
117	and parsers (e.g. `parse_config_kind`) but owns its own clap
118	attributes.
119	
120	## Consumer function shape
121	
122	Helper functions called by a subcommand body should accept the
123	relevant leaf type by reference rather than unpacking individual
124	fields at the call site:
125	
126	```rust
127	// Multi-field leaf — pass the whole leaf
128	fn run_retry(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path,
129	             retry: &PushRetryOptions) -> Result<…> { … }
130	
131	run_retry("git", &["push", …], cwd, &args.provision.push_retry)?;
132	```
133	
134	This wins on:
135	- Readability — `&args.provision.push_retry` reads as a single
136	  unit; the function body works with `retry.push_retries` (no
137	  leaf-name doubling because the parameter name is the consumer's
138	  choice).
139	- Argument count — every multi-field leaf collapses N args into
140	  one ref, so chained helpers don't accumulate
141	  `clippy::too_many_arguments` warnings.
142	- Future-proofing — extending a leaf with another field is a
143	  zero-touch change at every call site.
144	
145	For **single-field leaves** (e.g. `SquashOption`, `DryRunFlag`,
146	`PrivateFlag`), direct read at the consumer site
147	(`args.squash.value`, or `args.provision.dry_run.dry_run` for the
148	pre-`value` leaves) is fine — wrapping the inner value in a
149	`&LeafType` parameter doesn't earn the indirection.
150	
151	## Marker traits
152	
153	Three Bundle markers — one per content classification. Bundles
154	implement exactly one. Leaves do **not** implement these markers
155	(their category lives in the type-name suffix).
156	
157	- `FlagBundle: clap::Args` — pure-boolean bundle (every
158	  constituent leaf is a Flag). Rare in practice.
159	- `OptionBundle: clap::Args` — pure-non-boolean bundle (every
160	  constituent leaf is an Option).
161	- `OptionFlagBundle: clap::Args` — mixed bundle (constituents
162	  include both Flag and Option leaves). Most common in practice.
163	
164	Two Parser traits — conditional contracts implemented only when
165	a leaf has explicit parsing logic (custom `value_parser`):
166	
167	- `FlagParser` — boolean-domain parser. Implemented only for
168	  boolean leaves that take a value form (`--flag=true|false`).
169	  Presence/absence flags need no impl; clap parses directly.
170	- `OptionParser` — non-boolean-domain parser. Implemented only
171	  for leaves with custom parsing logic (e.g. `ScopeParser`,
172	  `RepoParser`); bare `Option<String>` leaves need no impl.
173	
174	`FlagParser` and `OptionParser` share an identical method shape;
175	the difference is documentation (which value-domain the parser
176	targets).
177	
178	All five traits are documentation-level — clap derive doesn't
179	see them; the discipline is that every leaf and bundle declares
180	the appropriate ones explicitly.
181	
182	## Bundle marker discipline
183	
184	Bundle markers aren't compiler-checked against contents. If a
185	bundle's leaf set shifts category — e.g. a pure-Flag bundle gains
186	an Option leaf — you must update the marker by hand
187	(`FlagBundle` → `OptionFlagBundle`). No lint, no failure; silent
188	drift if forgotten. Worth a glance at the marker line whenever a
189	bundle's `#[command(flatten)]` set changes.
190	
191	## Pattern A worked example
192	
193	`init`'s `--config` field originally carried init-specific help
194	("Only valid with `--scope=por`..."). It currently flattens
195	`ConfigOption` for the generic help; the `--scope=por` constraint
196	surfaces in `init`'s preflight error. If the help-text generality
197	becomes a usability problem, init will switch to Pattern A as the
198	worked example.
199	
200	## Layout note
201	
202	OFs currently sit as flat `<name>.rs` files alongside this
203	README. Bundle modules carry the `_bundle` suffix
204	(e.g. `provision_bundle.rs`) so the leaf/bundle split is visible
205	in the directory listing without opening files. If an individual
206	OF accumulates enough rationale, edge-case detail, or examples
207	to outgrow doc-comments, it will graduate to a `<name>/mod.rs` +
208	`<name>/README.md` subdirectory layout. Mechanical when needed;
209	not done preemptively.
210	
```

=== scope.rs ===
//! `--scope` — code,bot|por target set selector.
//! See [options_flags](README.md) for shared architecture.

use clap::Args;

/// Typed value of `--scope` — the kinds of repo set a
/// subcommand can target.
///
/// - `CodeBot` (default) — dual-repo: code + `.claude` bot
///   session.
/// - `Por` — single repo (Plain Old Repo); no `.claude/`,
///   no `.vc-config.toml`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum ScopeKind {
    #[default]
    CodeBot,
    Por,
}

/// Parse the `--scope` value into a `ScopeKind`.
///
/// - Accepts `code,bot` / `bot,code` (commutative) and `por`.
/// - Standalone `code` or `bot` errors — these are
///   config-lookup keywords; subcommands using `ScopeKind`
///   have no config-driven sides to look up against.
pub fn parse_scope_kind(s: &str) -> Result<ScopeKind, String> {
    match s {
        "code,bot" | "bot,code" => Ok(ScopeKind::CodeBot),
        "por" => Ok(ScopeKind::Por),
        "code" | "bot" => Err(format!(
            "'--scope={s}' is not a valid scope kind — use 'code,bot' (dual) or 'por' (single)"
        )),
        _ => Err(format!(
            "'--scope={s}' is not recognized — expected 'code,bot' or 'por'"
        )),
    }
}

/// `OptionParser` impl for `--scope` (non-boolean domain).
/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either
/// `parse_scope_kind` directly or `ScopeParser::parse`.
pub struct ScopeParser;

impl super::OptionParser for ScopeParser {
    type Value = ScopeKind;

    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {
        parse_scope_kind(s)
    }
}

/// `--scope` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see
/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct ScopeOption {
    /// Scope — `code,bot` (dual, default) or `por` (single).
    #[arg(
        long,
        short,
        value_name = "SCOPE",
        value_parser = parse_scope_kind,
        default_value = "code,bot",
        verbatim_doc_comment
    )]
    pub scope: ScopeKind,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_code_bot() {
        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("code,bot").unwrap(), ScopeKind::CodeBot);
    }

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_bot_code_commutative() {
        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("bot,code").unwrap(), ScopeKind::CodeBot);
    }

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_por() {
        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("por").unwrap(), ScopeKind::Por);
    }

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_code_alone_errors() {
        let err = parse_scope_kind("code").unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.contains("not a valid scope kind"), "got: {err}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_bot_alone_errors() {
        let err = parse_scope_kind("bot").unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.contains("not a valid scope kind"), "got: {err}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn scope_kind_unknown_errors() {
        let err = parse_scope_kind("xyz").unwrap_err();
        assert!(err.contains("not recognized"), "got: {err}");
    }
}
=== repo.rs ===
//! `--repo` — pick a repo target via the user-config
//! account chain. See [options_flags](README.md) for shared
//! architecture.

use clap::Args;

use crate::config::RepoSelector;

/// Parse the `--repo` value into a `config::RepoSelector`.
///
/// - `<cat>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: None }`.
/// - `<cat>=<val>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: Some(val) }`.
/// - Empty input, empty category, or empty value (after `=`)
///   errors.
pub fn parse_repo_arg(s: &str) -> Result<RepoSelector, String> {
    let s = s.trim();
    if s.is_empty() {
        return Err("--repo: value is empty".into());
    }
    match s.split_once('=') {
        Some((cat, val)) => {
            let cat = cat.trim();
            let val = val.trim();
            if cat.is_empty() {
                return Err(format!("--repo: missing category in '{s}'"));
            }
            if val.is_empty() {
                return Err(format!("--repo: empty value in '{s}'"));
            }
            Ok(RepoSelector {
                category: cat.to_string(),
                value: Some(val.to_string()),
            })
        }
        None => Ok(RepoSelector {
            category: s.to_string(),
            value: None,
        }),
    }
}

/// `OptionParser` impl for `--repo` (non-boolean domain).
/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either
/// `parse_repo_arg` directly or `RepoParser::parse`.
pub struct RepoParser;

impl super::OptionParser for RepoParser {
    type Value = RepoSelector;

    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {
        parse_repo_arg(s)
    }
}

/// `--repo` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see
/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct RepoOption {
    /// Repo target — `<cat>` or `<cat>=<val>`.
    ///
    /// - `<cat>` looks up the value via the account chain
    ///   in the user config.
    /// - `<cat>=<val>` uses the literal value, no config
    ///   lookup needed.
    /// - Specific category meanings (`remote`, `local`,
    ///   …) depend on the consumer subcommand.
    #[arg(
        long,
        value_name = "CAT[=VAL]",
        value_parser = parse_repo_arg,
        verbatim_doc_comment
    )]
    pub repo: Option<RepoSelector>,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn repo_arg_category_only() {
        let sel = parse_repo_arg("remote").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sel.category, "remote");
        assert_eq!(sel.value, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn repo_arg_category_and_value() {
        let sel = parse_repo_arg("local=/tmp/fixtures").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sel.category, "local");
        assert_eq!(sel.value.as_deref(), Some("/tmp/fixtures"));
    }

    #[test]

=== squash.rs (the value convention) ===
//! `--squash SOURCE,TARGET` — pick the `jj squash --from/--into`
//! revision pair (e.g. `@,@-`). See [options_flags](README.md) for
//! shared architecture.

use clap::Args;

/// Parsed `--squash` value: the source and target revisions.
///
/// - Produced by [`SquashSpec::parse`] (also wired as the leaf's
///   `value_parser`, so clap rejects malformed input at parse time).
/// - Both halves are guaranteed non-empty.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SquashSpec {
    pub source: String,
    pub target: String,
}

impl SquashSpec {
    /// Parse a `SOURCE,TARGET` pair (e.g. `@,@-`); both halves
    /// must be non-empty.
    pub fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
        let parts: Vec<&str> = s.split(',').collect();
        if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {
            return Err(format!(
                "invalid --squash value '{s}': expected SOURCE,TARGET (e.g. @,@-)"
            ));
        }
        Ok(SquashSpec {
            source: parts[0].to_string(),
            target: parts[1].to_string(),
        })
    }
}

/// `OptionParser` impl for `--squash` (non-boolean domain).
/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either
/// `SquashSpec::parse` directly or `SquashSpecParser::parse`.
pub struct SquashSpecParser;

impl super::OptionParser for SquashSpecParser {
    type Value = SquashSpec;

    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {
        SquashSpec::parse(s)
    }
}

/// `--squash` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see
/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
///
/// - The single field is named `value` (the parsed value-side of
///   the option); the flag name comes from `#[arg(long = "squash")]`,
///   so the consumer reads `args.<leaf>.value` rather than doubling
///   the flag name.
/// - Bare `--squash` (no value) defaults to `@,@-`.
/// - `num_args = 0..=1` so the value form `--squash @,@-` also
///   works without quoting.
#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub struct SquashOption {
    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
    #[arg(
        long = "squash",
        value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET",
        value_parser = SquashSpec::parse,
        default_missing_value = "@,@-",
        num_args = 0..=1
    )]
    pub value: Option<SquashSpec>,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn squash_parse_valid() {
        let sq = SquashSpec::parse("@,@-").unwrap();
        assert_eq!(sq.source, "@");
        assert_eq!(sq.target, "@-");
    }

    #[test]
    fn squash_parse_invalid() {
        assert!(SquashSpec::parse("@").is_err());
        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(",").is_err());
        assert!(SquashSpec::parse("@,").is_err());
        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(",@-").is_err());
    }
}
=== common_args.rs (head) ===
//! `CommonArgs` — the shared arg set for the read-only commit-query
//! subcommands (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`): a `REVISION` /
//! `COMMITS` positional pair, `-r`/`--revision`, `-R`/`--repo`,
//! `-s`/`--scope`, `-n`/`--commits`, and the `-l`/`-L` inter-repo
//! label pair.
//!
//! A "bundle" in the inline-fields sense (cf. `provision_bundle`'s
//! flatten-of-leaves form): the fields aren't extracted into per-flag
//! leaves because none of them is reused outside these four — if one
//! ever is (e.g. a future command wants just `--revision`), extract
//! that field into a leaf and `#[command(flatten)]` it here. Consumers
//! flatten this and add their own extra `#[arg]` fields (`list`'s
//! `-w`/`--width`, `show`'s `-f`/`--files`). See
//! [options_flags](README.md) for shared architecture.

use std::path::PathBuf;

use clap::Args;

use crate::scope::{Scope, parse_scope_roles};

/// Shared CLI args for the read-only commit-query subcommands —
/// see [Bundle](README.md#architecture).
///
/// - `pos_rev` / `pos_count` — the `REVISION` / `COMMITS` positionals
///   (`common::resolve_spec` reconciles them with `-r` / `-n`).
/// - `revision` — `-r` / `--revision` (default `@`); `..` notation is
///   parsed downstream by `common::parse_dot_rev`.
/// - `repo` / `scope` — `-R PATH` overrides the workspace root, `-s
///   code|bot|code,bot` selects sides; they compose
///   (`common::resolve_repos`). Defaults preserve today's behavior:
///   no flag → `[.]`, `-R foo` alone → `[foo]`. `-s` alone resolves
///   against `find_workspace_root()`; `-R + -s` resolves against the
///   `-R` path. `-s` is keyword-only today (path-via-`-s` and the
///   `-s <path>,roles` workspace-root override are planned — see
///   `notes/todo.md`).
/// - `limit` — `-n` / `--commits`, caps the output.
/// - `label` / `no_label` — `-l` / `--label` (default `===`) and
///   `-L` / `--no-label`; `common::resolve_header` combines them.
#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct CommonArgs {
    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,

    /// Number of commits to show (per dotted side)
    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,

    /// Revision to query
    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    pub revision: String,

    /// Workspace root or single jj repo path [default: .]
    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
    pub repo: Option<PathBuf>,

    /// Side(s) to query; composes with --repo as workspace root
    #[arg(
        short = 's',
        long = "scope",
        value_name = "code|bot|code,bot",
        value_parser = parse_scope_roles
    )]
    pub scope: Option<Scope>,

    /// Number of commits to show
    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
    pub limit: Option<usize>,

    /// Custom label decoration between repos
    #[arg(
        short = 'l',
        long = "label",
        value_name = "TEXT",
        allow_hyphen_values = true,
        default_value = "==="
    )]
    pub label: String,
```

> AGENT

I have enough context. Here's my read on both, kept tight.

## Todo #1 — `value` field sweep

**Scope, concretely:** 7 leaves (`scope`, `repo`, `dry_run`, `private`, `account`, `config`, `use_template`); consumers are almost entirely `src/init/params.rs` (~7 lines) and `src/init/tests.rs` (~30 lines). Single-step `0.50.0` is the right fit — mechanical, no cross-cycle ambiguity.

**The one gotcha:** the README already calls it out — if two `value`-field leaves get flattened into the same struct, clap panics on duplicate arg id `value`. `ProvisionOptionFlagBundle` flattens `dry_run` + `private` today, so both will need `#[arg(id = "dry_run", long = "dry-run", …)]` / `#[arg(id = "private", long, …)]` once converted. Cheap fix, but the test suite must run after this one or it surfaces at runtime.

## Subcommand invocation trait — recommendation

**Step A first — trait + tiny dispatch, no linkme.** Define:

```rust
trait Subcommand {
    type Params;
    fn into_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;
    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params)
        -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>;
    fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool { false }   // chid/desc/list/show override
    fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool { false }  // finalize overrides
}
```

Each match arm collapses to `Commands::Chid(a) => dispatch(a, cli.log)`. The `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` peek-before-dispatch logic moves behind the trait too. You keep the exhaustiveness check; the enum is still the source of truth.

**Step B — linkme later, if it earns it.** linkme's win is "add a subcommand by adding a file, no edits to `main.rs`". You'd pay:
- Loss of `match` exhaustiveness (a forgotten registration is a runtime gap, not a compile error).
- Help-output ordering becomes link-order unless explicitly sorted.
- Macro-magic dependency, ~`inventory`-class.

Today the per-subcommand cost in `main.rs` is three touchpoints (mod decl, `Commands` variant, match arm). After Step A it's three touchpoints with each arm being one line — at which point linkme's reduction-from-three-to-one (drop the variant + arm, keep only the registration) is real but modest. I'd hold linkme back until the friction is *felt*, not predicted.

**One alternative worth knowing:** [`enum_dispatch`](https://docs.rs/enum_dispatch/) auto-generates trait dispatch off the enum — Step A without writing the match arms by hand, still compile-time exhaustive. Closer-fitting than linkme to what you're describing.

## Sequencing

These two are independent — #1 is in `options_flags/` + `init/`; the trait sweep is in `main.rs` + the 12 subcommand modules. I'd suggest landing #1 first (small, well-scoped, closes a documented loose end from 0.47.0), then a separate cycle for Step A.

Want me to pull #1 into the In Progress ladder as `0.50.0`?

Quick clarification before I touch `todo.md`:

**The trait does *not* replace #1.** They're orthogonal:
- **Trait sweep (0.50.0):** touches `main.rs` dispatch + adds a `Subcommand` impl in each of the 12 subcommand modules. Each arg-struct keeps whatever field shape it has today.
- **#1 (`value` field sweep):** touches the 7 leaves in `options_flags/` + their consumers (`init/params.rs`, `init/tests.rs`). The leaves are flattened *inside* arg-structs; the dispatcher never reads them.

**Recommendation:** keep them as separate cycles — disjoint file sets, disjoint review surface, neither blocks the other. #1 stays at the top of `## Todo` and is a natural `0.50.1` (or whatever lands first after 0.50.0). Folding them into one bundle just makes the cycle title ungainly without saving any work.

The trait sweep touches `main.rs` + 12 subcommand modules. **I'd recommend multi-step** so the trait shape can be validated on one worked-example port before the bulk sweep:

- `0.50.0-0`: plan + version bump + `Subcommand` trait skeleton in a new file (no behavior change yet); `notes/todo.md` gets the new In Progress ladder *and* the linkme + inventory entries appended to `## Todo`; `notes/chores-10.md` opens the cycle section.
- `0.50.0-1`: port one subcommand end-to-end (probably `chid` — smallest `try_from`, no special-casing). Validates the trait shape under real load.
- `0.50.0-2..N`: port the remaining 11 in groups. Suggest grouping by complexity:
  - simple `From` (no fallibility): `validate_desc`, `fix_desc`, `clone`, `init`, `symlink`, `sync`, `push`
  - `TryFrom`: `chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`, `finalize`
  - the trait method names (`into_params` vs `try_into_params`?) want to handle both — minor, but worth deciding by example.
- `0.50.0-K`: rework `main.rs` to use the trait + drop the per-arm match boilerplate; move `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` peek logic onto the trait. (Could happen incrementally as each subcommand is ported, or at the end — I lean end-of-cycle so intermediate commits stay buildable without the dispatcher being half-converted.)
- `0.50.0` close-out: drop dev suffix, move In Progress → Done.

**The linkme + inventory entries** land in `0.50.0-0`'s `notes/todo.md` so they're captured immediately, paired with the cycle whose discussion produced them.

## chore: open 0.49.0 — finish Migration A (0.49.0-0)

Commits: [[1]]

Multi-step. Finish Migration A: port the last four subcommands
`pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` → `pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params:
&XxxParams)`, same shape as the `0.48.0` sweep. `chid` / `desc`
/ `list` / `show` all `#[command(flatten)]` `common::CommonArgs`,
so the cycle also adds a shared clap-free `CommonParams` they
reuse. Mechanical; no behavior change.

(Superseded at 0.49.0-1 — the cycle was expanded to the full
"CommonArgs sweep", B-first; see the next section's `### Cycle
re-scope`. This section keeps the original narrow framing as the
record of what the 0.49.0-0 commit's plan was.)

### Per-step shape

- `common::CommonParams` (flat, clap-free): the resolved
  `DotSpec` + `Header` + raw `repos: Vec<PathBuf>`, with
  `impl From<&CommonArgs>` doing the `resolve_spec` /
  `resolve_header` work at the binary edge. The `..` parsing and
  `-l`/`-L` resolution move out of the subcommand bodies.
- `XxxParams`: flat struct embedding `CommonParams`, plus the
  subcommand's own fields — `list` adds `width: usize`, `show`
  adds `files: FileLimit` (the `--files` string is parsed at the
  boundary). `impl From<&XxxArgs>` (total) for `chid` / `desc` /
  `list`; `impl TryFrom<&ShowArgs>` for `show` (fallible —
  `FileLimit::parse`), mirroring `finalize`.
- `pub fn x(args)` → `pub fn x(_ctx: &Context, params:
  &XxxParams)`; `ctx` unused (uniform-signature placeholder, as
  in `symlink` / `validate-desc` / `fix-desc`).
- `main.rs` dispatch arm builds `Context::load(cli.log)` + the
  params; the `suppress_banner` match keeps reading
  `a.common.no_label` off the args (clap edge, unchanged).
- Tests: existing `XxxArgs` parse tests untouched; add a small
  "construct `XxxParams` directly" test per the worked-example
  precedent.

### Ladder (original — superseded)

Smallest first; `show` (the `TryFrom` + `FileLimit` parse) last.

- 0.49.0-0 plan + version bump + this section + todo ladder
  (current)
- 0.49.0-1 chid + introduce `CommonParams` in `common.rs`
- 0.49.0-2 desc
- 0.49.0-3 list
- 0.49.0-4 show
- 0.49.0 close-out — drop suffix, todo→Done, ARCHITECTURE.md
  Migration A table all-done (12/12)

This cycle is Migration A only. Migration B for these four
(folding `CommonArgs` into the `options_flags/` leaf model,
dropping the repeatable `-R`/`--repo` for the `--scope` path
form) stays the separate "CommonArgs sweep" todo.

### Per-substep contract

Per `notes/substep-protocol.md`: `cargo fmt` / `clippy
--all-targets -- -D warnings` / `test` / `install --path .
--locked` + retest before each commit; bump `Cargo.toml` at
sub-step start; flip todo ladder markers; pair commits across
both repos with ochid trailers.

**No code in `-0`** — refinement from my earlier outline: the trait skeleton moves to `-1` so it lands with the first port (`chid`). Reason: a defined-but-unused trait would need `#[allow(dead_code)]` to pass clippy, and decoupling the trait shape from a real consumer for one commit's worth of life isn't worth it.

**Draft of the new chores section:**

```markdown
## chore: open Subcommand trait sweep (0.50.0-0)

Multi-step. The 12 subcommand match arms in `main.rs` repeat
the same `Context::load` + `try_from(&args)` + `run_command`
boilerplate — ~12 lines per arm. This cycle introduces a
`Subcommand` trait so each arm collapses to a single
`dispatch::<T>(args, cli.log)` line while keeping the
`Commands` enum as the dispatch source of truth (compile-time
exhaustiveness preserved). Plan-only opener; the trait + first
worked-example port land in `-1`.

### Trait shape (initial sketch)

- `Subcommand` trait on each subcommand's `Args` type:
  - `type Params`
  - `fn into_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>`
    — total `From` impls return `Ok(…)`; fallible `TryFrom`
    impls forward their error.
  - `fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params)
    -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>`
  - `fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool { false }` — `chid` /
    `desc` / `list` / `show` override.
  - `fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool { false }` —
    `finalize` overrides.
- Free helper `dispatch<S: Subcommand>(args: &S, log: …)
  -> ExitCode` wraps the per-arm body.
- `main.rs` match arms collapse to one line each; the
  banner / detached-exec peek logic moves behind the trait.

Shape is subject to refinement once `-1` exercises it on
`chid`.

### Ladder

- 0.50.0-0 plan + version bump + this section + todo ladder
  + linkme/inventory todos (current)
- 0.50.0-1 add `subcommand.rs` (trait) + port `chid`
  (worked example)
- 0.50.0-2..N port remaining 11 subcommands. Grouping
  decided per substep; candidate split:
  - `From` (total): `validate_desc`, `fix_desc`, `clone`,
    `init`, `symlink`, `sync`, `push`
  - `TryFrom` (fallible): `desc`, `list`, `show`, `finalize`
- 0.50.0-K `main.rs` dispatch rework — drop the per-arm
  match boilerplate; move banner / detached-exec peeks
  behind the trait.
- 0.50.0 close-out — drop suffix, todo→Done.

### Why not linkme / inventory now

Both eliminate the `Commands` enum entirely (link-time
distributed slice or runtime registry). They'd cut
per-subcommand touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant +
match arm) to 1 (registration). Costs: compile-time
exhaustiveness check goes away (missing registration =
runtime gap); help-output ordering becomes link-order
unless sorted; either is macro-magic crate. The trait sweep
gets every match arm to one line with none of those costs;
linkme + inventory are queued as separate Todo items to
revisit if the per-arm cost ever feels burdensome.

### Per-substep contract

Per `notes/substep-protocol.md`: `cargo fmt` / `clippy
--all-targets -- -D warnings` / `test` / `install --path .
--locked` + retest before each commit; bump `Cargo.toml` at
sub-step start; flip todo ladder markers; pair commits
across both repos with ochid trailers.
```

**Draft of the two new `## Todo` items** (inserted right after #1):

```markdown
1. **Investigate `linkme` for subcommand registration.**
   Distributed-slice registry — each subcommand registers
   itself at link time; `main.rs` discovers them via the
   slice rather than matching a `Commands` enum. Reduces
   per-subcommand touchpoints from 3 to 1. Cost: loses
   compile-time exhaustiveness (missing registration =
   runtime gap); help-output ordering depends on link
   order unless sorted; macro-magic dependency. Revisit
   once the `0.50.0` trait sweep's per-arm cost has been
   felt under real "add a subcommand" load.
   <https://github.com/dtolnay/linkme>
1. **Investigate `inventory` as `linkme` alternative.**
   Same shape as `linkme` — runtime-iterable registry
   populated by `inventory::submit!` per subcommand.
   Trade-offs mirror linkme's. Pick one if/when the
   trait sweep's match is the actual bottleneck.
   <https://github.com/dtolnay/inventory>
```

OK to proceed with these edits exactly as drafted, or adjust first?

  "new_string": "- 0.49.0 close-out — drop suffix, todo→Done (Context+Params\n  port 12/12 + CommonArgs sweep), README + ARCHITECTURE.md (done) [[12]]\n\n## chore: open Subcommand trait sweep (0.50.0-0)\n\nMulti-step. The 12 subcommand match arms in `main.rs` repeat\nthe same `Context::load` + `try_from(&args)` + `run_command`\nboilerplate — ~12 lines per arm. This cycle introduces a\n`Subcommand` trait so each arm collapses to a single\n`dispatch::<T>(args, cli.log)` line while keeping the\n`Commands` enum as the dispatch source of truth (compile-time\nexhaustiveness preserved). Plan-only opener; the trait + first\nworked-example port land in `-1`.\n\n### Trait shape (initial sketch)\n\n- `Subcommand` trait on each subcommand's `Args` type:\n  - `type Params`\n  - `fn into_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>`\n    — total `From` impls return `Ok(…)`; fallible `TryFrom`\n    impls forward their error.\n  - `fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params)\n    -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>`\n  - `fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool { false }` — `chid` /\n    `desc` / `list` / `show` override.\n  - `fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool { false }` —\n    `finalize` overrides.\n- Free helper `dispatch<S: Subcommand>(args: &S, log: …)\n  -> ExitCode` wraps the per-arm body.\n- `main.rs` match arms collapse to one line each; the\n  banner / detached-exec peek logic moves behind the trait.\n\nShape is subject to refinement once `-1` exercises it on\n`chid`.\n\n### Ladder\n\n- 0.50.0-0 plan + version bump + this section + todo ladder\n  + linkme/inventory todos (current)\n- 0.50.0-1 add `subcommand.rs` (trait) + port `chid`\n  (worked example)\n- 0.50.0-2..N port remaining 11 subcommands. Grouping\n  decided per substep; candidate split:\n  - `From` (total): `validate_desc`, `fix_desc`, `clone`,\n    `init`, `symlink`, `sync`, `push`\n  - `TryFrom` (fallible): `desc`, `list`, `show`, `finalize`\n- 0.50.0-K `main.rs` dispatch rework — drop the per-arm\n  match boilerplate; move banner / detached-exec peeks\n  behind the trait.\n- 0.50.0 close-out — drop suffix, todo→Done.\n\n### Per-step evaluation\n\nEffectiveness of the trait approach is evaluated after each\nsubstep. Possible outcomes at any step boundary:\n\n- Continue as planned.\n- Significantly modify the trait shape (e.g. swap\n  `into_params`/`run` for a single `dispatch` method on the\n  trait; absorb the `Args` type as an associated type instead\n  of `impl Subcommand for ArgsType`; etc.) — recorded in a\n  new chores subsection at the next step.\n- Abandon: revert the cycle to a non-trait baseline (close\n  the cycle as a no-op with a chores `### Outcome` note\n  capturing what didn't fit). The version-bump and todo\n  entries land in Done as the record of \"tried, didn't\n  ship.\"\n\nThis is part of the cycle's contract, not an exceptional\nexit path — the trait sweep is the lower-risk reading of\n\"reduce per-arm boilerplate\" and `linkme` / `inventory` are\nthe higher-leverage / higher-cost alternatives queued as\nfollow-up todos.\n\n### Why not linkme / inventory now\n\nBoth eliminate the `Commands` enum entirely (link-time\ndistributed slice or runtime registry). They'd cut\nper-subcommand touchpoints from 3 (mod decl + enum variant +\nmatch arm) to 1 (registration). Costs: compile-time\nexhaustiveness check goes away (missing registration =\nruntime gap); help-output ordering becomes link-order\nunless sorted; either is macro-magic dependency. The trait\nsweep gets every match arm to one line with none of those\ncosts; linkme + inventory are queued as separate Todo items\nto revisit if the per-arm cost ever feels burdensome.\n\n### Per-substep contract\n\nPer `notes/substep-protocol.md`: `cargo fmt` / `clippy\n--all-targets -- -D warnings` / `test` / `install --path .\n--locked` + retest before each commit; bump `Cargo.toml` at\nsub-step start; flip todo ladder markers; pair commits\nacross both repos with ochid trailers.\n\n# References"

```
## refactor: chid → Context+Params (0.49.0-3)

Commits: [[8]]

First of the four `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` Context+Params
ports (the chid leg of the cycle's Context+Params half — see
`### Context+Params port` at the top). The Args/Params layering
arrives at the read-only commit-query subcommands and introduces
the shared `CommonParams` the next three reuse.

- `common::CommonParams` (flat, clap-free): resolved `DotSpec` +
  `Header` + `repos: Vec<PathBuf>`. `impl TryFrom<&CommonArgs>`
  does the `resolve_spec` / `resolve_header` / `resolve_repos`
  work at the binary edge — fallible because `resolve_repos`
  can fail (workspace lookup, path issues).
- `chid::ChidParams`: flat struct embedding `CommonParams`,
  nothing else (chid has no fields beyond `CommonArgs`).
  `impl TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` delegates to
  `CommonParams::try_from`.
- `pub fn chid(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams)` — `ctx`
  unused (uniform-signature placeholder, as in
  `symlink` / `validate-desc` / `fix-desc`).
- `main.rs` dispatch builds `Context::load(cli.log)` +
  `ChidParams::try_from(&chid_args)` (mirrors the `Finalize`
  arm, the other `TryFrom` site).
- Tests: existing `ChidArgs` parse tests untouched; new
  `params_from_args_defaults` exercises the boundary
  resolution.

### Design: error type and import direction

Two small calls worth recording.

- **Error type `String`** on `TryFrom<&CommonArgs>` matches
  `finalize` (the existing fallible precedent), not
  `Box<dyn std::error::Error>`. The actual fallibility is
  `resolve_repos`'s `Box<dyn Error>` — coerced via
  `map_err(|e| e.to_string())` at the boundary. `String` keeps
  the param-construction error surface uniform across the four
  ports.
- **`common.rs` now imports
  `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`** to write the
  `TryFrom` impl — a new edge from `common` to `options_flags`.
  `options_flags/common_args.rs` already imports
  `crate::common::resolve_repos`, so the two modules now
  cross-reference within the crate. Not a layering inversion:
  `CommonParams` is the resolved/domain side, `CommonArgs` is
  the CLI-surface side, and the conversion is a one-way edge
  from CLI to domain. Defining the impl next to `CommonParams`
  (the target) is the natural place.
```

> AGENT

Plan for `-1`:

1. Cycle-start: backfill `0.50.0-0`'s `Commits: [[13]]` ref (SHA `040aa28804211e529baa4ebf0a27f3ebfcef6e95`).
2. Cargo.toml: `0.50.0-0` → `0.50.0-1`.
3. todo.md ladder flip: `-0 (current)` → `-0 (done)`, `-1 (current)`.
4. New `src/subcommand.rs` with the `Subcommand` trait.
5. `src/chid.rs`: `impl Subcommand for ChidArgs`.
6. `src/main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + import + chid arm + suppress_banner peek for chid.
7. New chores-10 section `## refactor: Subcommand trait + chid port (0.50.0-1)`.

**Naming decision:** The trait wants to be called `Subcommand`, but `clap::Subcommand` is imported in `main.rs` (for the derive on `enum Commands`). Cleanest fix: qualify the clap derive at the use site (`#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]`), drop `Subcommand` from the `use clap::{...}` line, and import ours plainly. Three options were on the table; I'll explain in the chores section.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/subcommand.rs",
  "content": "//! `Subcommand` trait — collapse per-arm dispatch boilerplate in\n//! `main.rs`. Each subcommand's `Args` type implements\n//! `Subcommand`; the trait's default `dispatch` method does the\n//! `Context::load` → `to_params` → `run` → `ExitCode` plumbing,\n//! so each `Commands::Foo(args) => …` arm collapses to a single\n//! `args.dispatch(cli.log)` call.\n//!\n//! - `to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>` — total\n//!   `From` impls return `Ok(...)`; fallible `TryFrom` impls\n//!   forward their error. Uniform `String` error type matches\n//!   the existing `CommonParams::try_from` / `FinalizeParams`\n//!   precedent.\n//! - `run(ctx, params)` is an associated function (no `&self`) —\n//!   `params` carries everything the body needs.\n//! - `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` — `false`-default\n//!   peek hooks. `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` override\n//!   `suppress_banner`; `finalize` overrides `is_detached_exec`.\n//!\n//! Introduced in the `0.50.0` cycle; `chid` is the worked\n//! example. The trait coexists with `clap::Subcommand` (the\n//! derive macro on `enum Commands`) — `main.rs` qualifies the\n//! clap derive at the use site (`#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]`)\n//! to free the unqualified `Subcommand` name for this trait.\n\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::process::ExitCode;\n\nuse log::error;\n\nuse crate::context::Context;\n\n/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so\n/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(cli.log)`\n/// call instead of repeating the load / params / run / exit\n/// boilerplate.\npub trait Subcommand: Sized {\n    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body\n    /// consumes.\n    type Params;\n\n    /// Build `Params` from `Args` at the binary edge. The\n    /// `Result` shape absorbs both `From` (return `Ok(...)`)\n    /// and `TryFrom` (forward the error) impls uniformly.\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;\n\n    /// Run the subcommand body.\n    fn run(\n        ctx: &Context,\n        params: &Self::Params,\n    ) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;\n\n    /// Whether to suppress the leading banner line. Default\n    /// `false`; subcommands with a `-L` / `--no-label`\n    /// script-friendly mode override.\n    fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool {\n        false\n    }\n\n    /// Whether this invocation is the detached\n    /// `finalize --exec` re-entry. Default `false`; `finalize`\n    /// overrides.\n    fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool {\n        false\n    }\n\n    /// Default dispatch: `Context::load(log)` → `to_params(&self)`\n    /// → `run(&ctx, &params)` → `ExitCode`. Replaces the per-arm\n    /// boilerplate in `main.rs`.\n    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {\n        let ctx = match Context::load(log) {\n            Ok(c) => c,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        let params = match self.to_params() {\n            Ok(p) => p,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        match Self::run(&ctx, &params) {\n            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                ExitCode::FAILURE\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

```
1	//! `chid` subcommand — print the short change ID of each commit in a
2	//! revision range, one per line (script-friendly output).
3	//!
4	//! - `ChidArgs`: clap surface; flattens
5	//!   `options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs`.
6	//! - `ChidParams`: clap-free; embeds `common::CommonParams`. Built
7	//!   via `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>` at the binary edge.
8	//! - `chid(&Context, &ChidParams)`: the op — `for_each_repo` + print
9	//!   `format_chid`.
10	
11	use clap::Args;
12	use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
13	use log::{debug, info};
14	
15	use crate::common::{self, CommonParams};
16	use crate::context::Context;
17	use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
18	
19	/// CLI args for `chid` — just the shared read-only commit-query args.
20	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
21	pub struct ChidArgs {
22	    #[command(flatten)]
23	    pub common: CommonArgs,
24	}
25	
26	/// Clap-free params for `chid`; embeds resolved `CommonParams`.
27	#[derive(Debug)]
28	pub struct ChidParams {
29	    pub common: CommonParams,
30	}
31	
32	impl TryFrom<&ChidArgs> for ChidParams {
33	    type Error = String;
34	
35	    /// Resolve clap `ChidArgs` into `ChidParams` by delegating to
36	    /// `CommonParams::try_from`; `chid` has no fields beyond
37	    /// `CommonArgs`.
38	    fn try_from(a: &ChidArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {
39	        Ok(ChidParams {
40	            common: CommonParams::try_from(&a.common)?,
41	        })
42	    }
43	}
44	
45	/// Print the short change ID of each commit in the resolved range.
46	///
47	/// `_ctx` is unused (chid has no user-config or `--log` consumer);
48	/// it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.
49	pub fn chid(_ctx: &Context, params: &ChidParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
50	    debug!("chid: enter");
51	    let c = &params.common;
52	    common::for_each_repo(&c.repos, &c.header, |workspace, repo| {
53	        let (ids, _) = common::collect_ids(
54	            workspace,
55	            repo,
56	            &c.spec.rev,
57	            c.spec.desc_count,
58	            c.spec.anc_count,
59	        )?;
60	        for commit_id in &ids {
61	            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
62	            info!("{}", common::format_chid(&commit));
63	        }
64	        Ok(())
65	    })?;
66	    debug!("chid: exit");
67	    Ok(())
68	}
69	
70	#[cfg(test)]
71	mod tests {
72	    use std::path::PathBuf;
73	
74	    use clap::Parser;
75	
76	    use crate::options_flags::common_args::CommonArgs;
77	    use crate::{Cli, Commands};
78	
79	    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> CommonArgs {
80	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
81	        match cli.command {
82	            Commands::Chid(a) => a.common,
83	            _ => panic!("expected Chid"),
84	        }
85	    }
86	
87	    #[test]
88	    fn defaults() {
89	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid"]);
90	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@");
91	        assert_eq!(c.repo, None);
92	        assert_eq!(c.scope, None);
93	        assert_eq!(c.limit, None);
94	    }
95	
96	    #[test]
97	    fn with_revision() {
98	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-r", "@-"]);
99	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@-");
100	    }
101	
102	    #[test]
103	    fn with_repo() {
104	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-R", ".claude"]);
105	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from(".claude")));
106	    }
107	
108	    #[test]
109	    fn with_scope_code() {
110	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
111	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "code"]);
112	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])));
113	    }
114	
115	    #[test]
116	    fn with_scope_code_bot() {
117	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
118	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "code,bot"]);
119	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])));
120	    }
121	
122	    #[test]
123	    fn with_repo_and_scope_compose() {
124	        // `-R` and `-s` compose: the path is the workspace root, the
125	        // roles are resolved within it. Both fields parse cleanly.
126	        use crate::scope::{Scope, Side};
127	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-R", "../foo", "-s", "bot"]);
128	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from("../foo")));
129	        assert_eq!(c.scope, Some(Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot])));
130	    }
131	
132	    #[test]
133	    fn scope_path_rejected_with_hint() {
134	        // Path-via-`-s` is a planned feature; today rejected with a
135	        // hint pointing at `-R`/`--repo`.
136	        let err = crate::Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "chid", "-s", "./foo"])
137	            .unwrap_err()
138	            .to_string();
139	        assert!(err.contains("path"), "got: {err}");
140	        assert!(err.contains("--repo"), "got: {err}");
141	    }
142	
143	    #[test]
144	    fn with_limit() {
145	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-n", "5"]);
146	        assert_eq!(c.limit, Some(5));
147	    }
148	
149	    #[test]
150	    fn all_opts() {
151	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-r", "@--", "-R", ".claude", "-n", "3"]);
152	        assert_eq!(c.revision, "@--");
153	        assert_eq!(c.repo, Some(PathBuf::from(".claude")));
154	        assert_eq!(c.limit, Some(3));
155	    }
156	
157	    #[test]
158	    fn label_default() {
159	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid"]);
160	        assert_eq!(c.label, "===");
161	        assert!(!c.no_label);
162	    }
163	
164	    #[test]
165	    fn label_custom() {
166	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-l", "---"]);
167	        assert_eq!(c.label, "---");
168	    }
169	
170	    #[test]
171	    fn no_label() {
172	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "-L"]);
173	        assert!(c.no_label);
174	    }
175	
176	    #[test]
177	    fn positional_rev() {
178	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "@-"]);
179	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("@-".to_string()));
180	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, None);
181	    }
182	
183	    #[test]
184	    fn positional_rev_and_count() {
185	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "@..", "5"]);
186	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("@..".to_string()));
187	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, Some(5));
188	    }
189	
190	    #[test]
191	    fn positional_both_dots() {
192	        let c = parse(&["vc-x1", "chid", "..abcd..", "3"]);
193	        assert_eq!(c.pos_rev, Some("..abcd..".to_string()));
194	        assert_eq!(c.pos_count, Some(3));
195	    }
196	
197	    #[test]
198	    fn params_from_args_defaults() {
199	        // ChidParams::try_from goes through the binary-edge resolution:
200	        // resolve_spec + resolve_header + resolve_repos.
201	        use super::ChidParams;
202	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "chid"]).unwrap();
203	        let args = match cli.command {
204	            Commands::Chid(a) => a,
205	            _ => panic!("expected Chid"),
206	        };
207	        let params = ChidParams::try_from(&args).unwrap();
208	        // default: no flags → repos resolves to [.]
209	        assert_eq!(params.common.repos, vec![PathBuf::from(".")]);
210	        // default revision @
211	        assert_eq!(params.common.spec.rev, "@");
212	    }
213	}

{

  "old_string": "### Per-substep contract\n\nPer `notes/substep-protocol.md`: `cargo fmt` / `clippy\n--all-targets -- -D warnings` / `test` / `install --path .\n--locked` + retest before each commit; bump `Cargo.toml` at\nsub-step start; flip todo ladder markers; pair commits\nacross both repos with ochid trailers.\n\n# References",
  "new_string": "### Per-substep contract\n\nPer `notes/substep-protocol.md`: `cargo fmt` / `clippy\n--all-targets -- -D warnings` / `test` / `install --path .\n--locked` + retest before each commit; bump `Cargo.toml` at\nsub-step start; flip todo ladder markers; pair commits\nacross both repos with ochid trailers.\n\n## refactor: Subcommand trait + chid port (0.50.0-1)\n\nWorked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the\n`Subcommand` trait and validate its shape on `chid`. The\n12-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` block in\n`main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to a single\n`args.dispatch(cli.log)` call. No other arms change — the\nremaining 11 ports follow in `-2..N` after the per-step\nevaluation gate.\n\n- New `Subcommand` trait (`src/subcommand.rs`): associated\n  `Params` type; required `to_params(&self) -> Result<Params,\n  String>` (absorbs both `From` and `TryFrom` shapes\n  uniformly, matching the `String` error type set by\n  `finalize` / `chid`); required `run(ctx, params)` as an\n  associated function (no `&self` — `params` carries\n  everything the body needs); default `suppress_banner` /\n  `is_detached_exec` peek hooks (both `false`); default\n  `dispatch(&self, log)` that does the\n  `Context::load`→`to_params`→`run`→`ExitCode` plumbing.\n  Method name landed as `to_params` (not `into_params` per\n  the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads more truthfully).\n- `chid::ChidArgs impl Subcommand`: `Params = ChidParams`,\n  `to_params` delegates to the existing `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`,\n  `run` calls `chid::chid`, `suppress_banner` reads\n  `self.common.no_label`.\n- `main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::\n  Subcommand;`; the Chid arm collapses to `Commands::Chid(args)\n  => args.dispatch(cli.log)`; the `suppress_banner` peek for\n  Chid switches to `a.suppress_banner()`. Other 11 arms\n  unchanged.\n- Tests unchanged — existing `ChidParams::try_from` tests\n  cover the same code path; the trait adds no new logic.\n\n### Naming: `Subcommand` vs `clap::Subcommand`\n\nThe trait wants to be called `Subcommand`, but\n`clap::Subcommand` was already imported in `main.rs` for the\nderive on `enum Commands`. Three options were on the table:\n\n- **`SubcommandRunner` / similar rename** — no collision,\n  but verbose at every `impl … for FooArgs` site (12 future).\n- **`use subcommand::Subcommand as _;`** — idiomatic\n  trait-method-only import, but reads obscurely; two\n  `Subcommand`s in scope, one anonymous.\n- **Qualify clap's derive at use site** ← landed. The\n  unqualified name `Subcommand` is freed for this trait; the\n  one occurrence of `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]` is the\n  cost. Single small touch, both trait identities readable.\n\n### Evaluation hook\n\nPer the cycle's per-step evaluation gate (see the `-0`\nsection's `### Per-step evaluation`), this is the first\nopportunity to modify the trait shape significantly or\nabandon the cycle. The shape can still change at `-2`'s\nboundary based on how the chid port reads in review.\n\n# References"

> AGENT

**`-1` ready for evaluation. Stopping per your interrupt.**

Cargo cycle clean: fmt, clippy, 390 tests pass, `cargo install --locked` succeeded, `vc-x1 chid -L` dogfooded — banner correctly suppressed (no `vc-x1 0.50.0-1` line before the change ID), proving the `suppress_banner` trait method wires end-to-end.

## What landed

**New file `src/subcommand.rs`** (≈92 lines) — the `Subcommand` trait:
- Associated `type Params`.
- Required `fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>`.
- Required `fn run(ctx, params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>` (associated function, no `&self`).
- `fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool` — default `false`.
- `fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool` — default `false`. **Needed `#[allow(dead_code)]`** because no caller is wired yet (the existing `is_detached_exec` peek in `main.rs` still hard-matches `Commands::Finalize`; that move happens in `-K`).
- `fn dispatch(&self, log) -> ExitCode` — default impl; loads `Context`, calls `to_params`, calls `Self::run`, maps to `ExitCode`.

**`src/chid.rs`** — `impl Subcommand for ChidArgs`: `Params = ChidParams`, `to_params` delegates to existing `TryFrom`, `run` calls `chid`, `suppress_banner` reads `self.common.no_label`.

**`src/main.rs`** — the call-site collapse:
- Chid arm: **17 lines → 1 line**: `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),`
- `suppress_banner` peek for Chid: `a.common.no_label` → `a.suppress_banner()`.
- `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::Subcommand;`.
- Naming: `use clap::{..., Subcommand}` → dropped `Subcommand` from the import; the one site that needed it (`enum Commands` derive) qualified to `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]`.

**Notes** — `Cargo.toml` bumped to `0.50.0-1`; `notes/todo.md` ladder flipped; `notes/chores-10.md` got the `-1` section + the `0.50.0-0` `Commits: [[13]]` backfill.

## Surfaces worth your evaluation

1. **Trait shape — `to_params` + `run` + `dispatch` as a default method.** Reads as `args.dispatch(cli.log)` at the call site. The `run` is an associated function (no `&self`); is that the right call, or would `&self` be more uniform? (Tradeoff: `&self` in `run` means access to args from inside the body, but `Params` already carries everything that survived the `to_params` boundary; passing both is redundant.)

2. **`is_detached_exec` dead-code allow.** Will recur as more methods get added to the trait before their callers; can be tolerated for the cycle, or we change the cadence (rework `main.rs` peeks in `-2` instead of `-K` so the trait methods get callers as they're added).

3. **Naming — `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]` qualification.** Lands one ugly use site to free the unqualified `Subcommand` name. Alternative reads (`SubcommandRunner`, `as _`) are queued in the chores `### Naming` subsection.

4. **`dispatch` as a default trait method vs free function `dispatch<S: Subcommand>(args, log)`.** Default-method reads better at call site (`args.dispatch(...)` vs `dispatch(&args, ...)`); but `Sized` constraint required on trait, and trait default methods are slightly less discoverable than a free function next to the trait. Acceptable trade?

5. **Boilerplate elimination, actual numbers.** Chid arm went 17→1. If the average across 11 remaining ports is similar, dispatcher gets ~140 lines smaller. Plus a one-time ~92-line trait file. Net ≈50 lines saved — but the bigger win is *uniformity* of the per-arm shape, not raw line count.

6. **The cycle's own complexity vs reward.** 12 ports × ~22 lines of impl each = ~264 lines of new impl code added. Net wash on line count; clear win on call-site uniformity. Worth continuing, or abandon and accept the per-arm boilerplate as the cost of explicit dispatch?

Awaiting your read. I won't draft a commit message until we've worked through these.
