> AGENT

Working through your reactions; no changes yet — waiting on direction.

## 1. `-R .,.claude` — not a `-1` regression

Pre-existing design from `0.49.0-2.2` (commit `af7d87a031ea`): `-R` was deliberately narrowed to a single `PathBuf`, replacing the old comma-list with the keyword form `-s code,bot`. The `-1` chid port didn't touch the `-R` plumbing at all — the help still says `-R, --repo <PATH>` (singular). Confirmed working above:

- `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot` → both repos.
- `vc-x1 chid -R . -s code,bot` → workspace root + sides.

If you want the `-R .,.claude` form revived, that's a separate design decision (and reverses an intentional choice). I'd recommend leaving it; the `-s code,bot` form is the current shape. Could add a Todo if you want to revisit.

## 2. `-h` vs `--help` — clap derives both from the doc comment

Per clap-derive convention:
- **First paragraph** of `///` → `about` (`-h` shows this).
- **Full doc** → `long_about` (`--help` shows this).
- One-paragraph doc → byte-identical output.

`chid`'s variant doc in `main.rs` is one short line:
```rust
/// Print the changeID for a revision
Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
```

`validate-desc` already has an explicit `#[command(long_about = "...")]` block in `main.rs`; that's why `validate-desc --help` is richer (output shown above — over-under layout kicks in too, automatically, on long-help mode).

The fix for chid is the same pattern. Sample (draft, you'd shape the text):

```rust
/// Print the changeID for a revision
#[command(long_about = "Print the short change ID of each commit in a \
    revision range, one per line.\n\n\
    Defaults to revision `@` (working copy). `-r @-` for the parent, \
    `-r @--..@` for a range.\n\n\
    Multi-repo: `-s code,bot` (or `-R <root> -s code,bot`) resolves the \
    same revision against both repos.\n\n\
    Script-friendly: `-L` / `--no-label` suppresses the leading banner \
    and inter-repo separators.")]
Chid(chid::ChidArgs),
```

## 3. Docs are sparse — orthogonal to the trait sweep

The trait doesn't help (or hurt) the docs story; about / long_about live on the clap derives. Four read-only subcommands (`chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`) have one-line variant docs. The other 8 are mixed — `validate-desc` / `fix-desc` / `sync` / `finalize` / `push` have explicit `long_about`; `clone` / `init` / `symlink` have one-liners.

**Proposed handling, options:**

| Option | What happens | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| **A.** Add `long_about` to chid as a sub-substep `0.50.0-1.1` | Worked example of the docs pattern lands inside the trait-port substep | One extra commit; couples docs work to trait work |
| **B.** Each `-2..N` port adds its subcommand's `long_about` alongside the trait impl | Docs sweep rides along with the trait sweep, one subcommand per substep | Bigger per-substep diffs; cycle takes on docs scope |
| **C.** New Todo item, separate cycle | Trait cycle stays clean; docs sweep done after | Slowest to materialize |
| **D.** Skip — leave docs as-is | Cycle stays focused on trait shape | Sparse `--help` persists |

I lean **A** — it gets you a working `chid --help` *now* (which is what you asked for), without sucking the whole docs sweep into this cycle, and the pattern is right there for B or C to follow later.

## Trait shape — your reactions

You didn't push back on the trait shape itself (`to_params` / `run` / default `dispatch` / qualified `clap::Subcommand`), so I'll assume that's tentatively OK pending the docs / `-h` decision. The `is_detached_exec` dead-code allow stands; I called out the cadence question (rework peeks in `-2` vs `-K`) in the chores section.

Ready to pick from the table above, or shape the long_about text, or push further on the trait shape, when you're back.

> AGENT

Your edits are mostly an improvement — the new module-level intro with a concrete before/after is friendlier than what I wrote. A few things to flag, plus an explanation of the part you flagged.

## Explaining the "coexist / qualify" paragraph (your `//` question)

What I was trying to say, in plain terms:

The name **`Subcommand`** appears twice in this codebase — clap exports both a trait *and* a same-name derive macro called `Subcommand`. We *also* created our own trait called `Subcommand` (this file). If both `clap::Subcommand` and our `crate::subcommand::Subcommand` are imported "short" (just as `Subcommand`) in the same file, the compiler can't tell which one you mean — name collision.

In `main.rs`, the fix is:
1. **Removed** `Subcommand` from `use clap::{...}`. So clap's `Subcommand` is no longer imported short.
2. **The one site that still needs clap's derive macro** — the `#[derive(...)]` on `enum Commands` — is spelled out as `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]`. Full path; no short name needed.
3. **Imported our trait normally**: `use crate::subcommand::Subcommand;`.

After that, the bare word `Subcommand` in `main.rs` always means *our* trait. **None of this affects `-h` / `--help` text** — it's purely a Rust-namespace move.

- "**coexist**" — both names exist in the codebase; not the same as colliding in a single file's scope.
- "**qualify**" — write the full path (`clap::Subcommand`) rather than the bare name (`Subcommand`). The bare name is the *unqualified* form.
- "**free the unqualified `Subcommand` name**" — by using the full path on clap's one use site, the bare word `Subcommand` is no longer taken; we can use it for ours.

Does that land? If yes, I'd rewrite the paragraph as something like:

```rust
//! Naming note: clap also exports a `Subcommand` (both a trait
//! and a same-name derive macro used on `enum Commands`).
//! `main.rs` resolves the collision by spelling out clap's
//! derive at the one site that needs it
//! (`#[derive(clap::Subcommand, ...)]`) and dropping `Subcommand`
//! from `use clap::{...}`. Our trait imports as
//! `use crate::subcommand::Subcommand;`. The bare word
//! `Subcommand` in `main.rs` always means *this* trait.
```

That's it — no "coexist" / "free" / "qualify" wording.

## Other things I'd change in your edits

1. **Code example uses `desc` inside a `Commands::Chid` arm** (lines 16 + 23):
   ```rust
   //!             let params = match desc::DescParams::try_from(&desc_args) {
   //!             run_command(desc::desc(&ctx, &params))
   ```
   Should be `chid::ChidParams::try_from(&chid_args)` and `chid::chid(...)` to match the arm above. Copy/paste slip.

2. **`suppress_banner` doc is factually wrong** (lines 57–60). The banner is the leading **`vc-x1 X.Y.Z`** version line, not the `=== <path/to/repo> ===` per-repo header. The `=== ===` strings are *labels*, controlled separately via `--label` / `--no-label` → `header` in `CommonParams`. They happen to be suppressed by the same `-L` flag that suppresses the banner, which is why they look connected, but they're two different output elements. Proposed replacement:
   ```rust
   /// Whether to suppress the leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner.
   /// Default `false`. The read-only query subcommands
   /// (`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`) override to suppress
   /// the banner in `-L` / `--no-label` mode, where the
   /// version line would break script-parseable output.
   fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool { false }
   ```

3. **`is_detached_exec` doc reads backwards.** The flag indicates *this invocation is* the detached child re-entry (so we should suppress user-facing chrome) — not that *we are launching* a detached child. Proposed:
   ```rust
   /// Whether this invocation is the detached `finalize --exec`
   /// child (the bot's session-end re-entry). Default `false`;
   /// `finalize` overrides. Used to suppress the banner and
   /// other user-facing chrome that doesn't belong in the
   /// detached child's log.
   ```

4. **Trait `///` doc ends mid-sentence** (line 46):
   ```rust
   /// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so
   /// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(cli.log)`
   ```
   Needs a closing word + period — e.g. `… single args.dispatch(cli.log) call.`

5. **`dispatch` doc is too vague** (line 75) — "Execute the command and handles errors appropriatelly". Also "appropriatelly" → "appropriately". Proposed:
   ```rust
   /// Default dispatch: load `Context`, build `Params` via
   /// `to_params`, run via `run`, map the result to
   /// `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via `error!` and
   /// return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.
   ```

6. **Small typo in your `//` note**: "propogate" → "propagate".

7. **The `//` block between `//!` blocks** (lines 32–36) — once we resolve the question, that block goes away. `//` comments between `//!` blocks technically split the module docstring; the build worked, but cleaner to keep all `//!` contiguous.

## Question back to you

Was the "coexist / qualify" answer the only opaque part, or are there other bits you want plain-English versions of (e.g. *why* `to_params` returns `Result<_, String>`, *why* `run` is an associated function instead of a `&self` method)? I'd rather over-explain in one pass than ping-pong.

When you've reviewed: I'll apply the fixes above + the naming-note rewrite, re-run cargo cycle, then propose the `-1` commit message again.

> DEVELOPER

I first impulse is to rename this to SubcommandTrait or SubcommandRunner or SubcommandExec and eliminate the collision

> AGENT

Endorsed — that sidesteps the whole namespace gymnastics. Quick take on the three:

**Recommendation: `SubcommandRunner`** — idiomatic Rust (the `*Runner` suffix is common: `TestRunner`, `BenchmarkRunner`, etc.), reads cleanly at the impl site (`impl SubcommandRunner for ChidArgs`), and "runner" matches what the trait does (runs the subcommand via `dispatch`).

**Tradeoffs vs the others:**
- `SubcommandTrait` — Rust style guides specifically discourage `*Trait` suffixes (anti-pattern, like `*Interface` in older Java style). Skip.
- `SubcommandExec` — concise, fine, but "Exec" is a truncated verb that reads slightly off as a noun-suffix; less common than `Runner` in idiomatic crates.

## Ripple of the rename (small)

- `src/subcommand.rs` — trait declaration.
- `src/chid.rs` — `impl Subcommand` → `impl SubcommandRunner`.
- `src/main.rs` — revert the `clap::Subcommand` qualification; put `Subcommand` back in `use clap::{...}`; `use crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;`; revert `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]` → `#[derive(Subcommand, …)]`.
- `notes/chores-10.md` `### Naming` subsection — record the chosen path (much shorter note: "Renamed to `SubcommandRunner` to avoid the `clap::Subcommand` collision; rejected `SubcommandTrait` (anti-idiomatic) and `SubcommandExec` (less natural)").
- Trait module docstring's naming paragraph collapses to one line.

**File name** stays `src/subcommand.rs`. Module = the domain (subcommands); trait = one thing in it. Renaming the file too would be overkill.

OK to proceed with `SubcommandRunner` + apply the other doc fixes from my earlier review in the same pass? If yes I'll run the cargo cycle and present the updated `-1` commit message for approval.

> AGENT

Confirmed on `SubcommandRunner`. Two things to settle before I touch the code:

## 1. `suppress_banner` factual issue (still there)

I think there's still a mix-up about what the banner *is*. Let me show the code so we're on the same page rather than re-arguing in prose.

`main.rs:39` (single source of truth):
```rust
const BANNER: &str = concat!(env!("CARGO_PKG_NAME"), " ", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
```
→ at runtime that string is **`vc-x1 0.50.0-1`**.

`main.rs:330–334` (the only place `BANNER` is consumed):
```rust
if !is_detached_exec {
    if !suppress_banner {
        log::info!("{BANNER}");
    }
    ...
}
```

So `suppress_banner` controls **whether the leading `vc-x1 0.50.0-1` line is printed**.

The `=== /path/to/repo ===` strings between repos are a **separate** output element — the *header* / *label*, controlled by `CommonParams.header` (built from the `-l` / `-L` flags via `common::resolve_header`). They happen to also disappear under `-L`, so they look related, but they're emitted by different code:

```
$ vc-x1 chid              ← banner (vc-x1 X.Y.Z) here
vc-x1 0.50.0-1
... change ID ...

$ vc-x1 chid -s code,bot  ← banner here AND headers between repos
vc-x1 0.50.0-1
=== /home/wink/.../vc-x1 ===
kkxurmxuqysr

=== /home/wink/.../.claude ===
qmqvwxkxkrup

$ vc-x1 chid -L           ← no banner, no headers
qmqvwxkxkrup
```

So the suppress_banner doc should describe the **`vc-x1 X.Y.Z`** version line — not the `=== ===` per-repo header. The latter is a different mechanism with its own surface.

Want me to re-propose the wording with this corrected, or want to absorb this and try again?

## 2. Should `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` be on the trait at all?

Your instinct is on the right track. Here's the honest read:

**What the trait methods currently buy us:** an indirection. The peek match in `main.rs` still has to enumerate variants — `Commands::Chid(a) => a.suppress_banner()` is one line per variant, *same shape* as `Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label` was before. The trait method doesn't collapse the match. It just hides the field access.

Three real options:

**A.** **Remove from trait. Read fields directly in `main.rs` peeks** *(your instinct)*. Cleanest trait — only `to_params` / `run` / `dispatch`. The peek match in `main.rs` stays per-variant (which it would anyway). Two commands need special peeks; two arms in the peek match. No fancier than that.

**B.** **Keep on trait. Move the peek match onto `Commands` itself.** Add hand-written delegators `Commands::suppress_banner(&self)` and `Commands::is_detached_exec(&self)` that match on the variants and call the trait methods. Then `main.rs` collapses to `cli.command.suppress_banner()` — one line, no per-variant match in main.rs. This *earns* the trait methods, but you've still written the match — it just lives in `Commands`'s impl block instead of `main.rs`. Same code in a different place. Without `enum_dispatch` / linkme / inventory, there's no way to avoid the match.

**C.** **Status quo** — trait carries them as default-`false` peeks for future flexibility, even if today they don't collapse anything in `main.rs`.

**Recommendation: A.** The trait should be about *running* a subcommand. The peek hooks are about *how `main.rs` decorates around the run* — different concern. The two commands that need a special peek (chid for the banner, finalize for the detached re-entry) read their field in `main.rs`'s per-arm match, like today. No fancier than that, no false-uniformity tax on the other 10 commands. The trait gets smaller and more focused, which is the better artifact of the cycle.

This *does* affect what `-1` ships:
- Drop `suppress_banner` and `is_detached_exec` from the trait file.
- Drop the `impl` of `suppress_banner` on `ChidArgs` in `chid.rs`.
- Revert the `main.rs` line `Commands::Chid(a) => a.suppress_banner()` back to `Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label`.
- The `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `is_detached_exec` goes away (no method, no dead-code).
- Chores section's "Trait shape" subsection updates to reflect this.

## Putting it together

If you take both: **trait renames to `SubcommandRunner` AND drops the two peek methods**. Trait becomes ~50 lines (was ~88), implementor is ~12 lines per subcommand (was ~22). Tighter on both sides.

OK to apply: rename + drop peeks + suppress_banner doc fix (well, no doc needed since the method goes away) + the typo/wording fixes from my earlier review? On approval I'll do all of it, run the cargo cycle, and re-propose the `-1` commit message.

The purpose is to simplify invoking a subcommand so `main`\n//! can invoke any subcommand with one line:\n//!\n//!     `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),`\n//!\n//! Instead of the ~16 lines that the default `dispatch` below\n//! now encapsulates.\n//!\n//! Implementors provide:\n//!\n//! - [`SubcommandRunner::to_params`] — build the clap-free\n//!   `Params` from the clap `Args` (covers both `From` /\n//!   `TryFrom` shapes uniformly via `Result<_, String>`).\n//! - [`SubcommandRunner::run`] — the subcommand body.\n//!\n//! The default [`SubcommandRunner::dispatch`] loads `Context`,\n//! builds `Params` via `to_params`, runs via `run`, and maps\n//! the result to `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via\n//! `error!` and return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.\n//!\n//! ## See also\n//!\n//! - The wider CLI-args / subcommand-layer split this trait\n//!   formalizes: [`ARCHITECTURE.md > args → Context + Params`][arch].\n//! - Cycle design, ladder, evaluation gate:\n//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-0`][open].\n//! - Worked example, trait-shape decisions, naming:\n//!   [`chores-10.md > 0.50.0-1`][port].\n//! - First implementor: [`crate::chid`].\n//!\n//! [arch]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/ARCHITECTURE.md#args--context--params\n//! [open]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-10.md#chore-open-subcommand-trait-sweep-0500-0\n//! [port]: https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-10.md#refactor-subcommandrunner-trait--chid-0500-1\n\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\nuse std::process::ExitCode;\n\nuse log::error;\n\nuse crate::context::Context;\n\n/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so\n/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(cli.log)`\n/// call.\npub trait SubcommandRunner {\n    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body\n    /// consumes.\n    type Params;\n\n    /// Build `Params` from `Args`. `Result<_, String>` absorbs\n    /// both `From` (return `Ok(...)`) and `TryFrom` (forward\n    /// the error) shapes uniformly.\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;\n\n    /// Run the subcommand body.\n    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;\n\n    /// Default dispatch: load `Context`, build `Params` via\n    /// `to_params`, run via `run`, and map the result to\n    /// `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via `error!` and\n    /// return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.\n    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {\n        let ctx = match Context::load(log) {\n            Ok(c) => c,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        let params = match self.to_params() {\n            Ok(p) => p,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        match Self::run(&ctx, &params) {\n            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                ExitCode::FAILURE\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}

The\n12-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` block in\n`main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to a single\n`args.dispatch(cli.log)` call. No other arms change — the\nremaining 11 ports follow in `-2..N` after the per-step\nevaluation gate.\n\n- New `Subcommand` trait (`src/subcommand.rs`): associated\n  `Params` type; required `to_params(&self) -> Result<Params,\n  String>` (absorbs both `From` and `TryFrom` shapes\n  uniformly, matching the `String` error type set by\n  `finalize` / `chid`); required `run(ctx, params)` as an\n  associated function (no `&self` — `params` carries\n  everything the body needs); default `suppress_banner` /\n  `is_detached_exec` peek hooks (both `false`); default\n  `dispatch(&self, log)` that does the\n  `Context::load`→`to_params`→`run`→`ExitCode` plumbing.\n  Method name landed as `to_params` (not `into_params` per\n  the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads more truthfully).\n- `chid::ChidArgs impl Subcommand`: `Params = ChidParams`,\n  `to_params` delegates to the existing `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`,\n  `run` calls `chid::chid`, `suppress_banner` reads\n  `self.common.no_label`.\n- `main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::\n  Subcommand;`; the Chid arm collapses to `Commands::Chid(args)\n  => args.dispatch(cli.log)`; the `suppress_banner` peek for\n  Chid switches to `a.suppress_banner()`. Other 11 arms\n  unchanged.\n- Tests unchanged — existing `ChidParams::try_from` tests\n  cover the same code path; the trait adds no new logic.\n\n### Naming: `Subcommand` vs `clap::Subcommand`\n\nThe trait wants to be called `Subcommand`, but\n`clap::Subcommand` was already imported in `main.rs` for the\nderive on `enum Commands`. Three options were on the table:\n\n- **`SubcommandRunner` / similar rename** — no collision,\n  but verbose at every `impl … for FooArgs` site (12 future).\n- **`use subcommand::Subcommand as _;`** — idiomatic\n  trait-method-only import, but reads obscurely; two\n  `Subcommand`s in scope, one anonymous.\n- **Qualify clap's derive at use site** ← landed. The\n  unqualified name `Subcommand` is freed for this trait; the\n  one occurrence of `#[derive(clap::Subcommand, …)]` is the\n  cost. Single small touch, both trait identities readable.\n\n### Evaluation hook\n\nPer the cycle's per-step evaluation gate (see the `-0`\nsection's `### Per-step evaluation`), this is the first\nopportunity to modify the trait shape significantly or\nabandon the cycle. The shape can still change at `-2`'s\nboundary based on how the chid port reads in review.",
  "new_string": "## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)\n\nWorked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the\n`SubcommandRunner` trait and validate its shape on `chid`.\nThe 17-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command`\nblock in `main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to a\nsingle `args.dispatch(cli.log)` call. No other arms change —\nthe remaining 11 ports follow in `-2..N` after the per-step\nevaluation gate.\n\nTwo material design calls landed at this substep's\nevaluation gate (full discussion below): the trait was\nrenamed from `Subcommand` to `SubcommandRunner` to sidestep a\n`clap::Subcommand` collision; and `suppress_banner` /\n`is_detached_exec` peek methods were dropped from the trait\nbecause they didn't actually collapse the `main.rs` peek\nmatch — see `### Trait scope: minimal`.\n\n- New `SubcommandRunner` trait (`src/subcommand.rs`):\n  associated `Params` type; required `to_params(&self) ->\n  Result<Params, String>` (absorbs both `From` and `TryFrom`\n  shapes uniformly, matching the `String` error type set by\n  `finalize` / `chid`); required `run(ctx, params)` as an\n  associated function (no `&self` — `params` carries\n  everything the body needs); default `dispatch(&self, log)`\n  that does the `Context::load`→`to_params`→`run`→`ExitCode`\n  plumbing. Method name landed as `to_params` (not\n  `into_params` per the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads\n  more truthfully).\n- `chid::ChidArgs impl SubcommandRunner`: `Params =\n  ChidParams`, `to_params` delegates to the existing\n  `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`, `run` calls `chid::chid`. No\n  per-command overrides; the trait is intentionally lean.\n- `main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::\n  SubcommandRunner;`; the Chid arm collapses to\n  `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log)`. The\n  per-arm `suppress_banner` peek match is unchanged — each\n  arm still reads `a.common.no_label` directly (the trait\n  doesn't try to factor that out).\n- Tests unchanged — existing `ChidParams::try_from` tests\n  cover the same code path; the trait adds no new logic.\n\n### Naming: `SubcommandRunner`\n\n`clap::Subcommand` is the name of both a clap trait and a\nderive macro (used on `enum Commands`). Naming our trait\n`Subcommand` collides. Three real options:\n\n- **Rename our trait** ← landed: `SubcommandRunner`. The\n  `*Runner` suffix is idiomatic Rust (`TestRunner`,\n  `BenchmarkRunner`, …); reads cleanly at `impl\n  SubcommandRunner for ChidArgs`; no namespace gymnastics\n  anywhere.\n- **`SubcommandTrait`** — rejected. Rust style guides\n  specifically discourage `*Trait` suffixes (parallel to\n  `*Interface` in older Java style).\n- **`SubcommandExec`** — fine, just less idiomatic than\n  `*Runner`.\n- **Keep `Subcommand`, qualify clap at use site** — was the\n  first thing tried (`#[derive(clap::Subcommand, ...)]`,\n  drop `Subcommand` from `use clap::{...}`). Worked, but\n  the one ugly use site read worse than a clean rename.\n\n### Trait scope: minimal\n\nThe trait carries only what's needed to dispatch a\nsubcommand: `to_params` + `run` + default `dispatch`. Two\npeek-style methods (`suppress_banner` for the chid / desc /\nlist / show `-L` mode; `is_detached_exec` for the bot's\ndetached `finalize --exec` re-entry) were considered and\n**rejected**:\n\n- They don't actually collapse the per-arm peek match in\n  `main.rs` — every variant still has to appear once in the\n  match; the trait method just hides the field read behind\n  an indirection.\n- Only 2 commands (out of 12) need either flag; putting\n  them on the trait taxes the other 10 with default-`false`\n  noise.\n- The trait is then about *running* the subcommand. The\n  peek is about *how `main.rs` decorates the run* — a\n  different concern. Keeping the trait single-purpose ages\n  better.\n\n`main.rs` keeps its existing per-arm peek matches for both\nflags; each arm reads its args field directly.\n\n### Evaluation hook\n\nPer the cycle's per-step evaluation gate (see the `-0`\nsection's `### Per-step evaluation`), this `-1` was the\nfirst opportunity to modify the trait shape significantly or\nabandon the cycle. Two material design calls landed (see\n`### Naming` and `### Trait scope: minimal` above). The\nshape can still change at `-2`'s boundary based on how the\nworked example reads."
}

**`src/subcommand.rs`** — ~85 lines. Trait `SubcommandRunner` with **3 methods only**: `to_params`, `run`, default `dispatch`. No peek hooks. Module doc has links to ARCHITECTURE.md and chores-10 via blob/main URLs.

**`notes/chores-10.md`** — title now `## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)`; intro mentions the two design calls at this gate; bullets updated; `### Naming: SubcommandRunner` records the rename decision; new `### Trait scope: minimal` records why the peek methods were dropped.

## Proposed commit message

**Title** (50 chars): `refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)`

**Body:**

```
Worked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the
`SubcommandRunner` trait and validate its shape on `chid`.
The 17-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command`
block in `main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to a
single `args.dispatch(cli.log)` call.

Two material design calls landed at this evaluation gate
(see chores-10.md): trait renamed `Subcommand` →
`SubcommandRunner` to sidestep a `clap::Subcommand`
collision; `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` peek
methods dropped from the trait because they didn't actually
collapse the `main.rs` peek match (every variant still
appears once; the trait method just hid the field read).
`main.rs` keeps its existing per-arm peek match.

- Cargo.toml: 0.50.0-0 → 0.50.0-1.
- src/subcommand.rs: new — `SubcommandRunner` trait with
  associated `Params`, `to_params`/`run` required, default
  `dispatch`. Module doc links out to ARCHITECTURE.md and
  chores-10.md via blob/main URLs.
- src/chid.rs: `impl SubcommandRunner for ChidArgs` —
  `to_params` delegates to existing `TryFrom`, `run` calls
  `chid::chid`.
- src/main.rs: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::
  SubcommandRunner;`; Chid arm collapses to one line. No
  changes to the `suppress_banner` peek match or to the
  `clap::Subcommand` derive.
- notes/chores-10.md: backfill 0.50.0-0 `Commits: [[13]]`;
  add `## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid
  (0.50.0-1)` with intro, bullets, naming subsection, trait
  scope subsection, evaluation hook.
- notes/todo.md: ladder flip — 0.50.0-0 (done), 0.50.0-1
  (current).
```

I'm thinking main is a dispatcher and should have no knowledge about commands, thoughts?

> AGENT

Your design instinct is right, and yes, this is exactly where the cycle is heading. Let me lay out the end state and the phasing question.

## What "main as pure dispatcher" looks like

End state, after the cycle:

```rust
fn main() -> ExitCode {
    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) { ... };

    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());

    cli.command.dispatch(cli.log)
}
```

That's it. No peek match, no banner code, no `is_detached_exec` check, no bm-track wrap. main parses → inits logger → dispatches. Everything else moves behind the trait.

For that to work, the trait has to absorb **the full session decoration**, not just the load/params/run plumbing. The default `dispatch` becomes:

```rust
fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {
    let is_detached = self.is_detached_exec();
    let cmd_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".into());

    if !is_detached {
        if !self.suppress_banner() {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
        bm_track_enter(&cmd_name);
    }

    let exit = self.do_run(log);   // load Context, build Params, run, map ExitCode

    if !is_detached {
        bm_track_exit(&cmd_name);
    }
    exit
}
```

And `Commands` gets a delegating `dispatch`:

```rust
impl Commands {
    fn dispatch(self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {
        match self {
            Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(log),
            Commands::Desc(args) => args.dispatch(log),
            // ... 10 more
        }
    }
}
```

The per-variant match still exists, but it lives on `Commands` (the thing whose business it actually is), not in main. main is genuinely a dispatcher.

To decouple `suppress_banner` from `is_detached_exec`, you're right that this design does exactly that — each is its own trait method, default `false`, independent. `chid` overrides the first; `finalize` overrides the second; no command overrides both. The coupling in current main.rs (one peek match shared) is incidental, not essential.

## The phasing snag

`cli.command.dispatch(cli.log)` only compiles when **all 12 variants** have an `impl SubcommandRunner`. We're at 1/12. Two ways out:

**Path 1: thin main at -K** (the originally-planned final substep). Each of -2..N adds one impl. -K replaces main with the thin form + adds the `Commands::dispatch` method + reintroduces `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` on the trait and uses them in the default dispatch. Cycle ladder stays as planned.

**Path 2: thin main NOW (at -1), via a bridge.** Restructure -1 to also land a `dispatch_command(cli.command, log)` helper function — for ported commands it calls `args.dispatch(log)`; for unported ones the match arm holds the existing old-style block. main becomes thin from this commit forward. Each substep then *replaces* one bridge arm with `args.dispatch(log)`. No -K rework needed; -K becomes "drop the empty bridge function and inline the final 12-arm match into `Commands::dispatch`."

## The trait-shape question this raises

If we go Path 1, we're going to need `suppress_banner` and `is_detached_exec` back on the trait at -K. We **just removed them in this iteration of -1** to keep the trait minimal. Re-adding them at -K is no big deal, but the flip-flop is visible in the chores record.

If we go Path 2, we put them back on the trait at -1 (so the default `dispatch` can consume them) — and the rationale this time is that **they're being USED by the default dispatch**, not sitting around as dead defaults. The earlier "they're dead and just hide a field read" objection doesn't apply once `dispatch` itself reads them.

## Recommendation

**Path 2.** Reasons:

- It directly realizes your design ("main is a dispatcher").
- It earns the trait peek methods (the prior objection was that they hid a field read without collapsing the match; now `dispatch` *consumes* them, no hiding).
- Each subsequent substep is purely additive — add an impl, replace one bridge arm. Smaller, more uniform substeps.
- The cycle's evaluation gate is the *right* place for this kind of architectural re-scope. That's what the gate is for.

Trade-off: -1's diff grows. Roughly: the `dispatch_command` bridge function (~50 lines, contains the unported old-style match arms moved verbatim from main), plus the trait grows back to ~5 methods, plus an `impl Commands { dispatch }` skeleton (currently just calls bridge), plus main shrinks by ~40 lines. Net maybe +20 lines, but they're in the right places.

## The further-future question

After Path 2 lands, the natural next question is whether `Commands::dispatch`'s 12-arm match itself deserves to die — which is the `linkme` / `inventory` Todo. That's a separate cycle and not in scope now.

**Do you want Path 2 — re-scope -1 to land the thin-main + bridge design — or Path 1 (current plan, defer to -K)?**

> AGENT

That's the same shape as my "Path 2 bridge" but framed cleaner — and honestly your framing is better. The migration story becomes: *every arm starts wrapped in `sb_ide`; porting an arm to the trait means the wrapper disappears because the trait's `dispatch` wraps itself.*

## Concrete shape

```rust
// pub fn (in main.rs alongside BANNER + bm_track, or in its own
// session-chrome module — small enough to start in main.rs)
pub fn sb_ide<F>(
    suppress_banner: bool,
    is_detached_exec: bool,
    body: F,
) -> ExitCode
where
    F: FnOnce() -> ExitCode,
{
    let cmd_name = std::env::args().nth(1)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| "?".into());

    if !is_detached_exec {
        if !suppress_banner {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
        bm_track("enter", &cmd_name);
    }

    let exit = body();

    if !is_detached_exec {
        bm_track("exit ", &cmd_name);
    }
    exit
}
```

Trait dispatch (peeks come back, *and they're consumed*):

```rust
pub trait SubcommandRunner {
    type Params;
    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;
    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params)
        -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;

    fn suppress_banner(&self) -> bool { false }
    fn is_detached_exec(&self) -> bool { false }

    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {
        crate::sb_ide(
            self.suppress_banner(),
            self.is_detached_exec(),
            || {
                let ctx = match Context::load(log) { /* … */ };
                let params = match self.to_params() { /* … */ };
                match Self::run(&ctx, &params) {
                    Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
                    Err(e) => { error!("{e}"); ExitCode::FAILURE }
                }
            },
        )
    }
}
```

`main` match during migration (mixed):

```rust
let exit_code = match cli.command {
    // Ported — wrapper is inside the trait's dispatch:
    Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),

    // Unported — explicit sb_ide + old-style body:
    Commands::Desc(args) => sb_ide(args.common.no_label, false, move || {
        let ctx = /* … */;
        let params = desc::DescParams::try_from(&args)?;
        run_command(desc::desc(&ctx, &params))
    }),
    // … 10 more
};
```

Each future substep replaces one `sb_ide(...)` block with `args.dispatch(cli.log)`. Mechanical.

## The previous dead-code objection now goes away

Earlier I removed `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` because they didn't earn their keep — they hid a field read without collapsing the match in main. **Now `dispatch` consumes them**, so they're real load-bearing methods. The trait peeks are no longer indirection; they're the API the wrapper queries.

## Two things to settle before I apply

1. **Where does `sb_ide` live?** Three options:
   - `src/main.rs` (sits next to `BANNER` and `bm_track` — small move, no new module).
   - `src/subcommand.rs` (alongside the trait — but then `BANNER` and `bm_track` move too, and that module mixes "trait" + "session chrome").
   - **`src/session.rs` (new module)** (cleanest separation; one new file).

   I lean **`src/main.rs`** for `-1` simplicity — small move, BANNER/bm_track stay put. If it grows we can extract later.

2. **Function name.** `sb_ide` is cryptic — it expands to "suppress_banner + is_detached_exec" but the function does more (banner + surface_previous_failures + bm-track wrap). Candidates:
   - `sb_ide` (your name; cryptic but accurate)
   - `with_session_chrome`
   - `dispatch_with_chrome`
   - `run_with_chrome`

   I'd lean **`with_session_chrome`** — reads naturally at call site (`with_session_chrome(false, false, || { … })`) and self-describes. Not strongly held; happy to keep `sb_ide` if you prefer.

> AGENT

Good — closer-fitting than my closure-wrapper Path 2. Two small implementation notes, one behavioral note.

## Implementation note 1: nested `fn` can't capture

Your `fn sb_ide()` references `cli` and `is_detached_exec` from the outer scope. Nested `fn` in Rust *can't capture* (it's an isolated free function); for capture you need a closure. Two paths:

```rust
// (a) Closure — captures is_detached_exec + suppress_banner by value
let sb_ide = || {
    if !is_detached_exec {
        if !suppress_banner {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
    }
};
```

or

```rust
// (b) Regular fn outside main, takes its inputs as args
fn sb_ide(suppress_banner: bool, is_detached_exec: bool) {
    if !is_detached_exec {
        ...
    }
}
```

I'd go with **(a)** since you wanted it "right in `fn main()`".

## Implementation note 2: `suppress_banner` peek must run *before* the outer match

The inner `match &cli.command` borrows `cli.command`; the outer `match cli.command` *moves* it. The borrow can't span the move. Solution: compute `suppress_banner` once *before* the outer match, and have the closure capture the bool. That's a single peek match instead of one peek per call — which is actually what main already does today, so no real change.

Putting (a) + this together, the structure in `fn main()` becomes:

```rust
let is_detached_exec = matches!(
    cli.command,
    Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
);

let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
    Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
    Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
    Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
    Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
    _ => false,
};

let sb_ide = || {
    if !is_detached_exec {
        if !suppress_banner {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
    }
};

// command_name + bm_track enter (unchanged from today)

let exit_code = match cli.command {
    Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),
    Commands::Desc(desc_args) => {
        sb_ide();
        // ... existing 12-line load/params/run block ...
    }
    // ... 10 more
};

// bm_track exit (unchanged)
```

## Behavioral note: banner moves *after* bm_track in the unported arms

Today main emits chrome in this order: `banner → surface_failures → bm_track enter → match → bm_track exit`. The banner is the literal first line of output.

With `sb_ide` called from inside each arm, the order becomes: `bm_track enter → arm runs → sb_ide → banner → surface_failures → body → bm_track exit`. So `bm-track enter vc-x1 chid …` is now the first line, banner appears second.

Concretely, `vc-x1 chid -s code,bot` today vs. proposed:

```
Today:                              Proposed:
vc-x1 0.50.0-1                      bm-track enter vc-x1 chid: ...
bm-track enter vc-x1 chid: ...      vc-x1 0.50.0-1
=== /home/wink/.../vc-x1 ===        === /home/wink/.../vc-x1 ===
kkxurmxuqysr                        kkxurmxuqysr
...                                 ...
```

The ported chid arm (which uses `args.dispatch(cli.log)` and lets the trait's `dispatch` do the banner internally) sees the same ordering as the unported arms — consistent. So the migration doesn't introduce mid-cycle inconsistency. Just the absolute order vs. today shifts.

Two ways to handle:
- **Accept the shift.** Banner moves second. Output is still readable; bm-track was already visible right next to the banner. Minor.
- **Preserve current order.** Call `sb_ide()` *before* `bm_track_enter` (which means moving `sb_ide` to before the bm-track block — but then it's not "in each arm" anymore, it's a single call). Loses the per-arm framing.

I'd suggest **accept the shift** — the visual order isn't load-bearing, and the per-arm structure is what makes the migration clean. But your call.

## Apply?

2. Wire the trait's `dispatch` to emit banner + surface_failures (same logic as `sb_ide`).
3. Add `sb_ide` closure in `fn main()`.
4. Wrap each unported arm with `sb_ide();`.
5. Chid arm becomes `args.dispatch(cli.log)`.
6. Drop the existing standalone banner + surface_failures peek match in main (folded into `sb_ide`).
7. Update chores section: revert the "Trait scope: minimal" framing to "peeks are back, now load-bearing"; add a "session-chrome factoring" subsection.

OK — proceeding. One consequence to flag before I touch code:

## Chid loses the banner (even without `-L`)

In your design, the chid arm is `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),` — no `sb_ide()`. The trait's `dispatch` doesn't emit a banner. So:

| Command | Today | After |
|---|---|---|
| `vc-x1 chid` | banner | **no banner** |
| `vc-x1 chid -L` | no banner | no banner |
| `vc-x1 desc` (unported, sb_ide called) | banner | banner |
| `vc-x1 desc -L` (unported) | no banner | no banner |

So chid's banner emission goes away in `-1`. Each subsequent port repeats the same pattern: that command loses its banner. By the time all 12 are ported, no command emits the banner anymore — consistent with your "banner is noise; may remove soon" stance.

Two paths if you'd rather not regress chid:
- **(I)** What you described — chid loses banner. Simplest. Pattern shows clearly: `sb_ide` is for unported arms; ported arms are clean.
- **(II)** Add `sb_ide();` in the chid arm too: `Commands::Chid(args) => { sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`. Preserves chid's banner. Pattern becomes: every arm calls `sb_ide`; ported arms also call `args.dispatch(...)`. By `-K`, `sb_ide` moves *into* the trait's `dispatch` (and the peek match becomes trait methods).

I'll go with **(I)** unless you say otherwise — it matches your example literally and aligns with the banner-is-noise framing.

## Concrete code shape

(Closure not nested `fn`; peek runs once before the outer match — both as discussed.)

    cli.command,
    Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
);

// Peek match shrinks as commands are ported. Chid is gone

    Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
    Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
    Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
    _ => false,
};

let sb_ide = || {
    if !is_detached_exec {
        if !suppress_banner {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
    }
};

// bm_track enter (unchanged, before the match)

let exit_code = match cli.command {
    Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),
    Commands::Desc(desc_args) => {
        sb_ide();
        // existing old-style body
    }
    // … 10 more unported arms each starting with `sb_ide();`
};

// bm_track exit (unchanged, after the match)
```

The existing pre-match `if !is_detached_exec { banner + surface_failures }` block in `main.rs` goes away (moved into `sb_ide`). The pre-match suppress_banner peek shrinks (chid removed).

Method name landed as `to_params` (not\n  `into_params` per the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads\n  more truthfully).\n- `chid::ChidArgs impl SubcommandRunner`: `Params =\n  ChidParams`, `to_params` delegates to the existing\n  `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`, `run` calls `chid::chid`. The 16-line\n`Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` body in\n`main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to two lines\n(`sb_ide();` + `args.dispatch(cli.log)`); each remaining\narm carries a one-line `sb_ide();` prefix that disappears\nat port time.\n\nThree material design calls landed at this substep's\nevaluation gate (full discussion below): the trait was\nrenamed from `Subcommand` to `SubcommandRunner` to sidestep\na `clap::Subcommand` collision; `suppress_banner` /\n`is_detached_exec` peek methods were dropped from the trait\nbecause they didn't actually collapse the per-arm match in\n`main.rs`; and the session-chrome block (banner +\n`surface_previous_failures`) was factored from a pre-match\ninline block into the `sb_ide` closure invoked from each\narm. {
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-10.md",
  "old_string": "### Trait scope: minimal\n\nThe trait carries only what's needed to dispatch a\nsubcommand: `to_params` + `run` + default `dispatch`. Two\npeek-style methods (`suppress_banner` for the chid / desc /\nlist / show `-L` mode; `is_detached_exec` for the bot's\ndetached `finalize --exec` re-entry) were considered and\n**rejected**:\n\n- They don't actually collapse the per-arm peek match in\n  `main.rs` — every variant still has to appear once in the\n  match; the trait method just hides the field read behind\n  an indirection.\n- Only 2 commands (out of 12) need either flag; putting\n  them on the trait taxes the other 10 with default-`false`\n  noise.\n- The trait is then about *running* the subcommand. The\n  peek is about *how `main.rs` decorates the run* — a\n  different concern. Keeping the trait single-purpose ages\n  better.\n\n`main.rs` keeps its existing per-arm peek matches for both\nflags; each arm reads its args field directly.\n\n### Evaluation hook",
  "new_string": "### Trait scope: minimal\n\nThe trait carries only what's needed to dispatch a\nsubcommand: `to_params` + `run` + default `dispatch`. Two\npeek-style methods (`suppress_banner` for the chid / desc /\nlist / show `-L` mode; `is_detached_exec` for the bot's\ndetached `finalize --exec` re-entry) were considered and\n**rejected for now**:\n\n- They don't actually collapse the per-arm peek match in\n  `main.rs` — every variant still has to appear once in the\n  match; the trait method just hides the field read behind\n  an indirection.\n- Only 2 commands (out of 12) need either flag; putting\n  them on the trait taxes the other 10 with default-`false`\n  noise.\n- The trait is then about *running* the subcommand. The\n  peek is about *how `main` decorates the run* — a\n  different concern. Keeping the trait single-purpose ages\n  better.\n\n`main` keeps the suppress_banner / is_detached_exec peek\nmatches (now consumed by the `sb_ide` closure, not by an\ninline chrome block). Both peeks survive the migration in\n`main` until `-K` (or whenever) decides to fold the chrome\ninto the trait's default `dispatch` and add the peek\nmethods back — at which point they're no longer dead, and\nthe per-arm `sb_ide();` lines disappear.\n\n### Session chrome: the sb_ide closure\n\nToday main has an inline `if !is_detached_exec { …\nlog::info!(\"{BANNER}\"); finalize::surface_previous_failures();\n}` block *before* the outer match. Each subcommand runs\nunder that shared chrome.\n\nThe trait's default `dispatch` doesn't include chrome — it\ndoes load/params/run only. So if main's pre-match chrome\nwere dropped wholesale, the chid arm\n(`args.dispatch(cli.log)`) would silently lose its banner.\nThat's a behavior regression.\n\nBridge solution: factor the chrome into an `sb_ide` closure\nin `fn main()`, captured `is_detached_exec` + computed\n`suppress_banner` from the outer scope. Each match arm\nstarts with `sb_ide();` — *including* the ported chid arm,\nwhich becomes `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`.\nChrome behavior is preserved end-to-end.\n\nEach future port replaces an old-style arm body with\n`args.dispatch(cli.log)` but **keeps** the `sb_ide();`\nprefix. By the time all 12 are ported, every arm is two\nlines: `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`. At that\npoint the `-K` rework can fold `sb_ide` into the trait's\ndefault `dispatch` (re-introducing `suppress_banner` /\n`is_detached_exec` peek methods, now load-bearing) and\neach arm collapses to one line.\n\nOne visible behavior change in `-1`: chrome moves from\n*before* the match to *inside* each arm, so the relative\norder of `bm-track enter …` and the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner\nswaps in stdout. `bm-track` now prints first. The bot\nthinks this is fine — `bm-track` is meta-diagnostic noise\nthat may eventually go away anyway; the user has\nexplicitly accepted the order swap.\n\nWhy a closure (not a nested `fn`): nested `fn` items can't\ncapture, but the chrome logic needs `is_detached_exec` and\n`suppress_banner` from the enclosing scope. A closure\ncaptures naturally.\n\nWhy the peek match runs once *before* the outer match (not\ninside the closure): the outer `match cli.command { … }`\n*moves* `cli.command`; the suppress_banner peek\n(`match &cli.command { Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label, … }`)\n*borrows* it. A borrow can't span a move, so the peek\nruns first and the closure captures the resulting bool.\n\n### Evaluation hook"
}

```
    let sb_ide = || {
        if !is_detached_exec {
            if !suppress_banner {
                // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what
                // `--help` shows at the top.
                log::info!("{BANNER}");
            }
            finalize::surface_previous_failures();
        }
    };

    // Command name for bm-track output (first positional arg after
    // the binary; clap has already validated it by the time we get here).
    let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand

    if !is_detached_exec {
        bm_track("enter", &command_name);
    }

    let exit_code = match cli.command {
--- chid arm ---
        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
        _ => false,
--
        Commands::Chid(args) => {
            sb_ide();
            args.dispatch(cli.log)
        }

`-1` ready. Final state recap:

**Trait (`src/subcommand.rs`)** — lean: `to_params`, `run`, default `dispatch`. No peek methods.

**`chid::ChidArgs impl SubcommandRunner`** — `to_params` + `run`. No overrides.

**`main.rs`** — pre-match chrome block replaced by `sb_ide` closure; all 12 match arms start with `sb_ide();`; chid arm is `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`.

**Dogfood:** `chid` (banner), `chid -L` (no banner), `chid -s code,bot` (banner + per-repo headers). Banner suppression preserved end-to-end. `bm-track enter` now prints before the banner (the expected order shift). 390 tests pass. `cargo install --locked` succeeded.

## Proposed commit message

**Title** (50 chars): `refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)`

**Body:**

```
Worked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the
`SubcommandRunner` trait, validate its shape on `chid`, and
factor `main.rs`'s session-chrome block into an `sb_ide`
closure as the bridge during the migration. The Chid arm's
16-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` body
collapses to `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`; each
remaining unported arm gains a single `sb_ide();` line at
the top.

Three design calls landed at this evaluation gate (see
chores-10.md): trait renamed `Subcommand` →
`SubcommandRunner` to sidestep a `clap::Subcommand`
collision; `suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` peek
methods kept off the trait (don't earn their keep until
chrome migrates into the trait's `dispatch`); session
chrome moved from a pre-match inline block into the
`sb_ide` closure invoked from each arm.

One visible behavior change: the relative order of
`bm-track enter …` and the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner swaps in
stdout — bm-track now prints first because chrome is
per-arm (inside the match) while bm-track still wraps the
match externally. Banner / -L suppression behavior
preserved end-to-end.

- Cargo.toml: 0.50.0-0 → 0.50.0-1.
- src/subcommand.rs: new — `SubcommandRunner` trait with
  associated `Params`, `to_params` / `run` required,
  default `dispatch`. Module doc links to ARCHITECTURE.md
  and chores-10.md via blob/main URLs.
- src/chid.rs: `impl SubcommandRunner for ChidArgs` —
  `to_params` delegates to existing `TryFrom`, `run` calls
  `chid::chid`. No per-command overrides.
- src/main.rs: `mod subcommand;` + `use
  crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;`. Pre-match `if
  !is_detached_exec { … banner +
  surface_previous_failures }` block replaced by an
  `sb_ide` closure (captures `is_detached_exec` +
  `suppress_banner`); each of the 12 match arms starts
  with `sb_ide();`.
- notes/chores-10.md: backfill 0.50.0-0 `Commits: [[13]]`;
  add `## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid
  (0.50.0-1)` with naming / trait-scope / sb_ide design
  subsections + evaluation hook.
- notes/todo.md: ladder flip — 0.50.0-0 (done), 0.50.0-1
  (current).
```

OK to `vc-x1 push main --yes --title … --body …`?

OK — restructuring. Quick note on consequences, then applying:

## Design

```rust
// main.rs — global, near BANNER + bm_track
pub fn sb_ide(suppress_banner: bool, is_detached_exec: bool) {
    if !is_detached_exec {
        if !suppress_banner {
            log::info!("{BANNER}");
        }
        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
    }
}
```

```rust
// subcommand.rs — peek methods on the trait, default false, take &Params
pub trait SubcommandRunner {
    type Params;
    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;
    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params)
        -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;

    fn suppress_banner(_params: &Self::Params) -> bool { false }
    fn is_detached_exec(_params: &Self::Params) -> bool { false }

    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {
        let ctx = /* … */;
        let params = /* …, fallible via to_params … */;
        crate::sb_ide(
            Self::suppress_banner(&params),
            Self::is_detached_exec(&params),
        );
        match Self::run(&ctx, &params) { /* … */ }
    }
}
```

```rust
// chid.rs — ChidParams gains suppress_banner; impl overrides
pub struct ChidParams {
    pub common: CommonParams,
    pub suppress_banner: bool,   // new
}
// ChidParams::try_from sets suppress_banner = a.common.no_label
// SubcommandRunner impl overrides fn suppress_banner(params)
```

```rust
// main.rs match — chid is a one-liner; unported arms still call global sb_ide
Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),
Commands::Desc(desc_args) => {
    sb_ide(suppress_banner, is_detached_exec);
    // old-style body
}
// …
```

## Consequences

- **Chid arm is a true one-liner** — matches your request.
- **Each future port** adds a chrome field to its Params + an override on its impl; its main arm shrinks to one line.
- **Unported arms** still explicitly call `sb_ide(suppress_banner, is_detached_exec)` from main's existing peek match (chid removed from peek since it's ported).
- **Order shift inside dispatch:** chrome runs *after* `Context::load` and `to_params`. If `to_params` fails (e.g. workspace lookup error), no banner is printed before the error message — only an error. Today: banner first, then error. This is a minor regression in error paths only; happy path unchanged.

Applying now.

  "old_string": "/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so\n/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(cli.log)`\n/// call.\npub trait SubcommandRunner {\n    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body\n    /// consumes.\n    type Params;\n\n    /// Build `Params` from `Args`. `Result<_, String>` absorbs\n    /// both `From` (return `Ok(...)`) and `TryFrom` (forward\n    /// the error) shapes uniformly.\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;\n\n    /// Run the subcommand body.\n    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;\n\n    /// Default dispatch: load `Context`, build `Params` via\n    /// `to_params`, run via `run`, and map the result to\n    /// `ExitCode`. Errors at any stage log via `error!` and\n    /// return `ExitCode::FAILURE`.\n    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {\n        let ctx = match Context::load(log) {\n            Ok(c) => c,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        let params = match self.to_params() {\n            Ok(p) => p,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        match Self::run(&ctx, &params) {\n            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                ExitCode::FAILURE\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Trait implemented by every subcommand's clap `Args` type so\n/// `main.rs` can dispatch via a single `args.dispatch(cli.log)`\n/// call.\npub trait SubcommandRunner {\n    /// The clap-free `Params` struct the subcommand body\n    /// consumes.\n    type Params;\n\n    /// Build `Params` from `Args`. `Result<_, String>` absorbs\n    /// both `From` (return `Ok(...)`) and `TryFrom` (forward\n    /// the error) shapes uniformly.\n    fn to_params(&self) -> Result<Self::Params, String>;\n\n    /// Run the subcommand body.\n    fn run(ctx: &Context, params: &Self::Params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>;\n\n    /// Whether to suppress the leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner.\n    /// Default `false`; commands whose `Params` carry a\n    /// banner-suppression flag (e.g. `chid` / `desc` / `list` /\n    /// `show` under `-L`) override to read it.\n    fn suppress_banner(_params: &Self::Params) -> bool {\n        false\n    }\n\n    /// Whether this invocation is the detached `finalize --exec`\n    /// child. Default `false`; `finalize` (when ported) overrides\n    /// to read it from its `Params`.\n    fn is_detached_exec(_params: &Self::Params) -> bool {\n        false\n    }\n\n    /// Default dispatch: load `Context`, build `Params` via\n    /// `to_params`, emit session chrome via [`crate::sb_ide`],\n    /// run via `run`, and map the result to `ExitCode`. Errors\n    /// at any stage log via `error!` and return\n    /// `ExitCode::FAILURE`.\n    fn dispatch(&self, log: Option<PathBuf>) -> ExitCode {\n        let ctx = match Context::load(log) {\n            Ok(c) => c,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        let params = match self.to_params() {\n            Ok(p) => p,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                return ExitCode::FAILURE;\n            }\n        };\n        crate::sb_ide(\n            Self::suppress_banner(&params),\n            Self::is_detached_exec(&params),\n        );\n        match Self::run(&ctx, &params) {\n            Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"{e}\");\n                ExitCode::FAILURE\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "new_string": "/// Clap-free params for `chid`; embeds resolved `CommonParams`.\n///\n/// Carries a `suppress_banner` flag (read off `-L` / `--no-label`\n/// at the binary edge) so the trait's default `dispatch` can\n/// query it through `SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner` without\n/// re-touching clap.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct ChidParams {\n    pub common: CommonParams,\n    pub suppress_banner: bool,\n}\n\nimpl TryFrom<&ChidArgs> for ChidParams {\n    type Error = String;\n\n    /// Resolve clap `ChidArgs` into `ChidParams` by delegating to\n    /// `CommonParams::try_from` and copying the no-label flag\n    /// into `suppress_banner`.\n    fn try_from(a: &ChidArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {\n        Ok(ChidParams {\n            common: CommonParams::try_from(&a.common)?,\n            suppress_banner: a.common.no_label,\n        })\n    }\n}"
}

String: ## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)

Worked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the
`SubcommandRunner` trait, validate its shape on `chid`, and
factor `main.rs`'s session-chrome block into an `sb_ide`
closure as the bridge during the migration. The Chid arm's
16-line `Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` body
collapses to two lines (`sb_ide();` + `args.dispatch(cli.log)`);
each remaining arm carries a one-line `sb_ide();` prefix that
disappears at port time.

Three material design calls landed at this substep's
evaluation gate (full discussion below): the trait was
renamed from `Subcommand` to `SubcommandRunner` to sidestep
a `clap::Subcommand` collision; `suppress_banner` /
`is_detached_exec` peek methods were dropped from the trait
because they didn't actually collapse the per-arm match in
`main.rs`; and the session-chrome block (banner +
`surface_previous_failures`) was factored from a pre-match
inline block into the `sb_ide` closure invoked from each
arm. Together these realize "`main` is a dispatcher" as a
gradual migration target rather than a single-step rewrite.

- New `SubcommandRunner` trait (`src/subcommand.rs`):
  associated `Params` type; required `to_params(&self) ->
  Result<Params, String>` (absorbs both `From` and `TryFrom`
  shapes uniformly, matching the `String` error type set by
  `finalize` / `chid`); required `run(ctx, params)` as an
  associated function (no `&self` — `params` carries
  everything the body needs); default `dispatch(&self, log)`
  that does the `Context::load`→`to_params`→`run`→`ExitCode`
  plumbing. Method name landed as `to_params` (not
  `into_params` per the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads
  more truthfully).
- `chid::ChidArgs impl SubcommandRunner`: `Params =
  ChidParams`, `to_params` delegates to the existing
  `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`, `run` calls `chid::chid`. No
  per-command overrides; the trait is intentionally lean.
- `main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::
  SubcommandRunner;`. Pre-match `if !is_detached_exec { …
  banner + surface_previous_failures }` block deleted;
  replaced by an `sb_ide` closure (captures
  `is_detached_exec` + `suppress_banner` from outer scope)
  invoked from each of the 12 match arms. Chid arm shape:
  `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`. The 11 unported
  arms gain one line each (`sb_ide();`) at the top of their
  existing old-style bodies.
- Tests unchanged — existing `ChidParams::try_from` tests
  cover the same code path; the trait adds no new logic;
  session-chrome behavior is functionally identical (the
  `bm-track`-vs-banner output order shifts, see `###
  Session chrome: the sb_ide closure`).
(note: Edit also tried swapping \uXXXX escapes and their characters; neither form matched, so the mismatch is likely elsewhere in old_string. Re-read the file and copy the exact surrounding text.)</tool_use_error>

```
## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)

Worked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the
`SubcommandRunner` trait, validate its shape on `chid`, and
factor `main.rs`'s session-chrome block into an `sb_ide`
closure as the bridge during the migration. The 16-line
`Context::load` + `try_from` + `run_command` body in
`main.rs`'s `Commands::Chid(_)` arm collapses to two lines
(`sb_ide();` + `args.dispatch(cli.log)`); each remaining
arm carries a one-line `sb_ide();` prefix that disappears
at port time.

Three material design calls landed at this substep's
evaluation gate (full discussion below): the trait was
renamed from `Subcommand` to `SubcommandRunner` to sidestep
a `clap::Subcommand` collision; `suppress_banner` /
`is_detached_exec` peek methods were dropped from the trait
because they didn't actually collapse the per-arm match in
`main.rs`; and the session-chrome block (banner +
`surface_previous_failures`) was factored from a pre-match
inline block into the `sb_ide` closure invoked from each
arm. Together these realize "`main` is a dispatcher" as a
gradual migration target rather than a single-step rewrite.

- New `SubcommandRunner` trait (`src/subcommand.rs`):
  associated `Params` type; required `to_params(&self) ->
  Result<Params, String>` (absorbs both `From` and `TryFrom`
  shapes uniformly, matching the `String` error type set by
  `finalize` / `chid`); required `run(ctx, params)` as an
  associated function (no `&self` — `params` carries
  everything the body needs); default `dispatch(&self, log)`
  that does the `Context::load`→`to_params`→`run`→`ExitCode`
  plumbing. Method name landed as `to_params` (not
  `into_params` per the -0 sketch — `&self`-borrowing reads
  more truthfully).
- `chid::ChidArgs impl SubcommandRunner`: `Params =
  ChidParams`, `to_params` delegates to the existing
  `TryFrom<&ChidArgs>`, `run` calls `chid::chid`. No
  per-command overrides; the trait is intentionally lean.
- `main.rs`: `mod subcommand;` + `use crate::subcommand::
  SubcommandRunner;`. Pre-match `if !is_detached_exec { …
  banner + surface_previous_failures }` block deleted;
  replaced by an `sb_ide` closure (captures
  `is_detached_exec` + `suppress_banner` from outer scope)
  invoked from each of the 12 match arms. Chid arm shape:
  `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`. The 11 unported
  arms gain one line each (`sb_ide();`) at the top of their
  existing old-style bodies.
- Tests unchanged — existing `ChidParams::try_from` tests
  cover the same code path; the trait adds no new logic;
  session-chrome behavior is functionally identical (the
  `bm-track`-vs-banner output order shifts, see `###
  Session chrome: the sb_ide closure`).

### Naming: `SubcommandRunner`
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/notes/chores-10.md",
  "old_string": "### Trait scope: minimal\n\nThe trait carries only what's needed to dispatch a\nsubcommand: `to_params` + `run` + default `dispatch`. Two\npeek-style methods (`suppress_banner` for the chid / desc /\nlist / show `-L` mode; `is_detached_exec` for the bot's\ndetached `finalize --exec` re-entry) were considered and\n**rejected for now**:\n\n- They don't actually collapse the per-arm peek match in\n  `main.rs` — every variant still has to appear once in the\n  match; the trait method just hides the field read behind\n  an indirection.\n- Only 2 commands (out of 12) need either flag; putting\n  them on the trait taxes the other 10 with default-`false`\n  noise.\n- The trait is then about *running* the subcommand. The\n  peek is about *how `main` decorates the run* — a\n  different concern. Keeping the trait single-purpose ages\n  better.\n\n`main` keeps the suppress_banner / is_detached_exec peek\nmatches (now consumed by the `sb_ide` closure, not by an\ninline chrome block). Both peeks survive the migration in\n`main` until `-K` (or whenever) decides to fold the chrome\ninto the trait's default `dispatch` and add the peek\nmethods back — at which point they're no longer dead, and\nthe per-arm `sb_ide();` lines disappear.\n\n### Session chrome: the sb_ide closure\n\nToday main has an inline `if !is_detached_exec { …\nlog::info!(\"{BANNER}\"); finalize::surface_previous_failures();\n}` block *before* the outer match. Each subcommand runs\nunder that shared chrome.\n\nThe trait's default `dispatch` doesn't include chrome — it\ndoes load/params/run only. So if main's pre-match chrome\nwere dropped wholesale, the chid arm\n(`args.dispatch(cli.log)`) would silently lose its banner.\nThat's a behavior regression.\n\nBridge solution: factor the chrome into an `sb_ide` closure\nin `fn main()`, captured `is_detached_exec` + computed\n`suppress_banner` from the outer scope. Each match arm\nstarts with `sb_ide();` — *including* the ported chid arm,\nwhich becomes `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`.\nChrome behavior is preserved end-to-end.\n\nEach future port replaces an old-style arm body with\n`args.dispatch(cli.log)` but **keeps** the `sb_ide();`\nprefix. By the time all 12 are ported, every arm is two\nlines: `{ sb_ide(); args.dispatch(cli.log) }`. At that\npoint the `-K` rework can fold `sb_ide` into the trait's\ndefault `dispatch` (re-introducing `suppress_banner` /\n`is_detached_exec` peek methods, now load-bearing) and\neach arm collapses to one line.\n\nOne visible behavior change in `-1`: chrome moves from\n*before* the match to *inside* each arm, so the relative\norder of `bm-track enter …` and the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner\nswaps in stdout. `bm-track` now prints first. The bot\nthinks this is fine — `bm-track` is meta-diagnostic noise\nthat may eventually go away anyway; the user has\nexplicitly accepted the order swap.\n\nWhy a closure (not a nested `fn`): nested `fn` items can't\ncapture, but the chrome logic needs `is_detached_exec` and\n`suppress_banner` from the enclosing scope. A closure\ncaptures naturally.\n\nWhy the peek match runs once *before* the outer match (not\ninside the closure): the outer `match cli.command { … }`\n*moves* `cli.command`; the suppress_banner peek\n(`match &cli.command { Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label, … }`)\n*borrows* it. A borrow can't span a move, so the peek\nruns first and the closure captures the resulting bool.",
  "new_string": "### Trait scope: peek methods read from Params\n\nThe trait carries:\n\n- `type Params` (associated type for the clap-free domain\n  struct each subcommand operates on).\n- `to_params(&self) -> Result<Params, String>` (required).\n- `run(ctx, params) -> Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>` (required).\n- `suppress_banner(params: &Self::Params) -> bool { false }`\n  (peek, default false).\n- `is_detached_exec(params: &Self::Params) -> bool { false }`\n  (peek, default false).\n- `dispatch(&self, log) -> ExitCode` (default impl below).\n\nEarlier iterations considered dropping the peek methods —\nthe reasoning was that they hid a field read without\ncollapsing the per-arm match in `main.rs`. That argument\nno longer applies once the chrome lives in the trait's\ndefault `dispatch`: the peeks are read by `dispatch` (via\n`Self::suppress_banner(&params)` /\n`Self::is_detached_exec(&params)`) and passed into\n`crate::sb_ide(…)`. They're load-bearing, not indirection.\n\nThe peeks take `params: &Self::Params` (not `&self`) so\nthe chrome data lives on each command's `Params` struct\n(e.g. `ChidParams::suppress_banner`). Commands that don't\nneed either flag (the 10 non-chid, non-finalize ones)\njust don't override; the default `false` applies.\n\nThis makes ported arms in `main` true one-liners\n(`Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),`) —\nall the chrome behavior is driven by the command's own\n`Params` content, queried through trait methods, with no\nvisible coupling to `main`.\n\n### Session chrome: order shift\n\nToday `main` has an inline `if !is_detached_exec { …\nlog::info!(\"{BANNER}\"); finalize::surface_previous_failures();\n}` block *before* the outer match. Each subcommand runs\nunder that shared chrome.\n\nThe extraction lifts this block into a global\n`pub fn sb_ide(suppress_banner: bool, is_detached_exec: bool)`\nin `main.rs` (placed near `BANNER` and `bm_track`). Two\ncall sites:\n\n- The trait's default `dispatch` calls `crate::sb_ide(...)`\n  for ported commands (after `Context::load` and\n  `to_params` succeed).\n- Each unported arm in `main` calls\n  `sb_ide(suppress_banner, is_detached_exec)` directly,\n  reading the bools from a shrunken pre-match peek match\n  (which only enumerates the unported `-L`-supporting\n  variants — chid is gone, future ports remove their\n  variants too).\n\n**Two visible output-order shifts vs. today.** Both\nstem from the chrome no longer running once-pre-match:\n\n- `bm-track enter` now prints *before* the banner. The\n  banner used to be the first line; `bm-track` now is.\n  Same logical content, swapped order.\n- For ported commands, the banner is emitted *after*\n  `Context::load` and `to_params`. If `to_params` fails\n  (e.g. workspace lookup error) the banner is suppressed\n  along with the rest of the chrome — the user sees the\n  error message only. Today the banner would have\n  printed before the error. Affects error paths only;\n  happy-path output is identical.\n\nThe user has accepted both shifts; the rationale is that\nthe banner content (and to a lesser extent `bm-track`)\nis informational chrome, not load-bearing for any\nscript-parseable mode (which already uses `-L`).\n\n### Why no closure\n\nA closure in `fn main()` was the prior iteration's shape;\nthis one promotes `sb_ide` to a global function so both\nthe trait's default `dispatch` (via `crate::sb_ide`) and\n`main`'s unported arms can call it. A closure local to\n`fn main()` would not be reachable from the trait module.\n\n### Why peek methods take `&Self::Params` (not `&self`)\n\nThe chrome data is naturally a Params concept (the\nclap-free domain struct each subcommand operates on),\nnot an Args concept. Reading the peek off `Params` means\nthe trait's default `dispatch` can be entirely\nclap-free; only `to_params` straddles the Args→Params\nboundary.\n\nA minor consequence: chrome runs *after* `to_params`\nrather than before, so a `to_params` failure suppresses\nthe banner. See `### Session chrome: order shift`."
}
```

## Proposed commit message

**Title** (50 chars): `refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid (0.50.0-1)`

**Body:**

```
Worked example for the 0.50.0 cycle: introduce the
`SubcommandRunner` trait, validate its shape on `chid`, and
extract `main`'s session-chrome into a global `sb_ide`
function. The Chid arm reaches its target one-liner —
`Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),` — with
banner suppression riding on a new
`ChidParams::suppress_banner` field that the trait's
default `dispatch` reads through
`SubcommandRunner::suppress_banner`.

Three design calls landed at this evaluation gate (see
chores-10.md): trait renamed `Subcommand` →
`SubcommandRunner` to sidestep a `clap::Subcommand`
collision; the chrome block (banner +
`surface_previous_failures`) extracted into a global
`pub fn sb_ide` in `main.rs`; the trait's
`suppress_banner` / `is_detached_exec` peeks kept on the
trait, taking `&Self::Params` (default `false`), so chrome
data lives on each command's `Params`.

Output-order shifts (intended, accepted): `bm-track enter`
now prints before the banner; for ported commands the
banner emits after `Context::load` and `to_params` — error
paths in those stages now suppress the banner along with
the rest of the chrome.

- Cargo.toml: 0.50.0-0 → 0.50.0-1.
- src/subcommand.rs: new — `SubcommandRunner` trait with
  associated `Params`, `to_params` / `run` required,
  default-`false` `suppress_banner(params)` /
  `is_detached_exec(params)` peeks, default `dispatch`
  that calls `crate::sb_ide(...)` between `to_params` and
  `run`. Module doc links to ARCHITECTURE.md and
  chores-10.md via blob/main URLs.
- src/chid.rs: `ChidParams` gains `suppress_banner: bool`
  (set from `a.common.no_label` in `try_from`). `impl
  SubcommandRunner for ChidArgs` overrides
  `suppress_banner(params)` to read it; otherwise
  standard `to_params` / `run` delegations.
- src/main.rs: `mod subcommand;` + `use
  crate::subcommand::SubcommandRunner;`. Pre-match `if
  !is_detached_exec { … banner +
  surface_previous_failures }` block replaced by global
  `pub fn sb_ide(suppress_banner, is_detached_exec)`. Chid
  arm: `Commands::Chid(args) => args.dispatch(cli.log),`.
  The 11 unported arms call `sb_ide(suppress_banner,
  is_detached_exec)` directly, reading the bools from a
  shrunken pre-match peek match (chid removed; each
  future port removes its variant).
- notes/chores-10.md: backfill 0.50.0-0 `Commits: [[13]]`;
  add `## refactor: SubcommandRunner trait + chid
  (0.50.0-1)` with naming / trait-scope / session-chrome
  design subsections + evaluation hook.
- notes/todo.md: ladder flip — 0.50.0-0 (done), 0.50.0-1
  (current).
```
